**Loader + the bounds question.** `InterpreterModelLoader` captures the `CustomFieldType.name` onto `FieldDef.customTypeName` (kind stays `Kind.STRING` — the value is string-shaped); it does **not** load the field's declared `minLength`/`maxLength` into a `StringType`-style length check, and that is **correct, not a regression** — the kernel does not generically enforce a custom type's bounds either (they are hints to the validator). The *registered* type's own `accepts` is the single source of truth on both engines; the field's declared bounds are metadata. (This retires the original IG7 worry — "the loader drops min/maxLength" — which would have been a *divergence* had we "fixed" it by enforcing the bounds directly.)

**Message-code boundary, corrected 2026-07-19.** The kernel emits the validator's **project-specific** error key, while the interpreter's Boolean SPI can emit only its constant `customFieldTypeInvalid`. The existing differential proves the downstream semantic effect—a rejected cell suppresses the referencing rule and an accepted cell lets it evaluate—but not the machine-observable formal-message code. That code-identity gap is now IG92; it is no longer described as exact Option-A parity. *Locked today:* `interpreter.CustomFieldTypeMultiplatformTest` (the three behaviours, JVM+JS) + `adapter.laws.CustomFieldTypeBoundsDiffTest` (registers the same length validator on both engines; suppression parity vs the real kernel).

### IF27 — Over-repetition structural check: `zuGrosseZeile` (group) / `zuGrosseKontextnummer` (field), with suppression (2026-06-26)

`validateFull` also runs a STRUCTURAL **over-repetition** check (KERNEL-FINDINGS "over-repetition errors", the kernel's `FormalChecker`): an entity instance whose repetition index exceeds the declared `repeatability` is flagged — a repeatable **group row** whose OWN index is over the cap → **`zuGrosseZeile`** (the group entity); a **field** sitting inside an over-limit repeatable *ancestor* (the over-limit level is intermediate) → **`zuGrosseKontextnummer`** (full field pointer). Both VALUE, on the `formalePruefung` path. An over-limit instance is **"unknown"**: its ordinary checks (format, mandatory, uniqueIndex, and any referencing rule) are suppressed.

The interpreter models it. `overLimitLevel(path, reps)` is the single predicate — the outermost repeatable ancestor whose row index exceeds [ModelDef.declaredReps] — and it threads through every pass: `overRepetitionMessages` emits one message per over-limit field (`zuGrosseKontextnummer`) and per over-limit group row (`zuGrosseZeile`); `computeFormalErrorAt` returns the over-repetition code for an over-limit cell (so a referencing rule goes UNKNOWN → suppressed, the single "is this operand unknown?" oracle, alongside format / required / dup-index); `formalErrors` skips an over-limit field (it carries the over-repetition code, not a format error); and `mandatoryErrors` + `duplicateIndexCells` skip over-limit rows. The group pointer falls out for free — [formatPointer] omits the trailing (group) segment's index, so an over-limit `/Order/Lines` row renders `/Order[1]/Lines`. The USB/multi-form `FEHLER_LAUFENDE_NR_ZU_GROSS` sibling is out of the single-document scope.

This is a deliberate **scope expansion**: the interpreter, a rule + computation evaluator, now also performs the kernel's structural document-shape validation, so `validateFull` output matches even on malformed-structure input (more rows than declared) — not just the structurally-valid documents a form UI produces. All in `commonMain` (JVM + JS). *Locked:* `adapter.laws.OverRepetitionDiffTest` — emission (flat + nested), rule suppression (a typed rulekit `Rule` via `TypedRules`), and mandatory + unique-index suppression on over-limit rows, all vs the real kernel.

### IF28 — condition/operation parser tolerates leading `;;` comments + all whitespace (FIXED 2026-06-26, graduated from IG18)

`;;` comments are kernel concrete syntax (KERNEL-FINDINGS "Condition comments"): the `Comment` token is grammatically valid as `before`/`after` of a `teilBedingung`/`operation`, and `WS` is `channel(HIDDEN)`, so the kernel parses past a leading comment (and any newline/tab) and evaluates the body at `validateFull`/`compute`. The interpreter's recursive-descent parser did **not** — `skipWs()` skipped only `' '`, so `readIdent` hit the `;` and threw `IllegalArgumentException`; worse, `validateWith`'s catch is `UnsupportedConstructException`-only, so the exception **escaped and crashed the whole run** (all four entry points — validateFull/validatePart/compute/evalCandidate — share this parser). This was a **crash**, not a wrong result.

The fix is the single shared locus: `skipWs()` now skips all whitespace (space/tab/newline/CR/`\u000C`, mirroring the kernel lexer's `WS`) and `;;` line comments (`;; …` to end of line, the `Comment` token). Because every meaningful parse position calls `skipWs()` first (`parsePrimary`, `parseAtom`), a commented condition/operation now parses straight to its body on every entry point. Deliberately **not** widened the `validateWith` catch — a genuine parse failure should still surface loudly (fail-fast), not be swallowed. *Locked:* `adapter.laws.CommentConditionDiffTest` — a DSL-authored body carrying a `;;` comment (attached via `Comments.before`, the model built through the real `EditSession` write path) fires identically on both engines.

### IF29 — STRING `noValueValidation` skips the whole per-cell format check (FIXED 2026-06-26, graduated from IG17)

`noValueValidation` is a StringType key the kernel honors at runtime (KERNEL-FINDINGS "StringType `noValueValidation`"): `FormatDefinitionString.validiereFormat` returns `Valid` immediately, so pattern / length / the default newline check are all bypassed. The interpreter ignored the key and enforced the constraints anyway — a **false positive** (`stringFalschesMuster`/`stringZuKurz`/`stringZuLang`/`stringMitNewline`) on a value the kernel accepts, reachable on shipped A12 Tools StringType fields (Country/City/Person).

The kernel only allows a *valid* `noValueValidation` field to carry `maxLength` (no pattern/minLength — `MVK_STRING_TYPE_PARAMS_INCONSISTENT`) and requires line breaks permitted (`MVK_STRING_TYPE_NO_VAL_AND_NO_LINE_BREAK`), so the single observable runtime effect is **a declared `maxLength` is NOT enforced**. The fix: `FieldDef`/`FieldConstraints` carry `noValueValidation`, `stringConstraintError` returns `null` immediately when set, and `InterpreterModelLoader` reads the StringType key. *Locked:* `adapter.laws.FieldConstraintFormalDiffTest` — a kernel-valid `noValueValidation` field (maxLength 6, line breaks allowed) leaves a length-7 value clean on both engines. (modelkit's `StringFieldBuilder.noValueValidation()` authors the key — DEV-API-GAPS DG14 — and fails fast on the line-break co-requirement.)

### IF30 — NUMBER integer-digit cap + value-string length formal checks (FIXED 2026-06-26, graduated from IG15)

Three NumberType keys the kernel formal-checks but the interpreter dropped (KERNEL-FINDINGS §3 supplement, kernel-probe-verified): **`maxIntegerDigits`** (integer-part digit count above it → `zahlZuvieleVorkommastellen`); **`trait:"amount"`** (an implicit integer-digit cap of `15 − scale`, only when no explicit `maxIntegerDigits` — ~~a distinct code `stelligkeitZuLang`~~ **corrected by IF67**: the amount cap fires the SAME `zahlZuvieleVorkommastellen`; `stelligkeitZuLang` is the separate generic >15-total-digit parse check — KERNEL-FINDINGS §3 "the two DISTINCT number-digit checks"); and NumberType **`minLength`/`maxLength`** on the value-string length → `zahlZuKurz`/`zahlZuLang`. The sign and fractional digits are excluded from the integer-digit count (`-1234.5` = 4); the length counts the full stored text (sign + decimal point). **This corrects the prior `KERNEL-FINDINGS`/`Model.kt` claim that these have "no DM-JSON key"** — `FieldtypeSerializer`'s `NumberTypeMixin @JsonProperty` proves they serialize. `trait:"amount"` is reachable through modelkit `NumberFieldBuilder.unit(NumberTrait.AMOUNT)`.

The fix: `FieldDef`/`FieldConstraints` carry `maxIntegerDigits` + a precomputed `amountMaxIntegerDigits` (`15 − scale`, set only when `trait:"amount"` and no explicit cap) + `numberMinLength`/`numberMaxLength`; `numberConstraintError` checks them; `InterpreterModelLoader` reads the keys (computing `amountTrait` from `trait == "amount"`). *Locked:* `adapter.laws.FieldConstraintFormalDiffTest` — one differential case per code, vs the real kernel.

### IF31 — 4-arg century `Date(day, month, century, year)` construction (FIXED 2026-06-26, graduated from IG12)

The kernel's `Date` construction has a 4-arg century form whose year is split into a 2-digit century + 2-digit year — full year = `century*100 + year` (KERNEL-FINDINGS "Date construction — 4-arg century form", kernel-probe-verified: `Date(15,6,20,24)` → year 2024). The interpreter's `analyzeDate` declined any non-3-arg form as `MALFORMED`, so `YearFromDate`/`Valid`/`Invalid` over a 4-arg date went `Unknown`/quiet — a **silent** divergence (the kernel evaluates a real date). Reachable via `Dsl.date/4` + the 4-arg `DslRenderer` emit; the construction's operand fields must carry a date-compatible NumberType range (the kernel rejects a plain field with `MVK_WRONG_DATE_FORMAT_FOR_OP`, and integer literals outright).

The fix is arity-flexible `analyzeDate`: it now accepts 3 OR 4 parts, computing the year as `century*100 + year` for the 4-arg form and reusing the existing empty/malformed/real-date logic — so every downstream extractor (`Year/Month/Day/QuarterFromDate`) and `Valid`/`Invalid` flow through unchanged. *Locked:* `adapter.laws.Date4ArgConstructionDiffTest` — the computed `YearFromDate` value (no hardcoded expectation) and `Valid` firing, both vs the real kernel.

### IF32 — every computation is also a validation rule: the implicit `[F] != <term>` check (FIXED 2026-06-26, graduated from IG14)

The kernel auto-generates, per computation, an implicit validation rule `FieldFilled(F) And [F] != <term>` that fires under the **computation's NAME** when a document's *stored* computed value disagrees with its term (KERNEL-FINDINGS "every computation is also a validation rule", locked kernel-side by `ComputationValidationLawsTest`). The interpreter's `validateWith` iterated only `model.rules`, so `validateFull`/`validatePart` never fired it — a silent miss whenever a stored value disagrees.

The fix synthesizes that rule and runs it through the SAME rule machinery (so the `!=` is the engine's own value-aware comparison, matching the kernel): `implicitComputationRule(comp)` builds `RuleDef(code = comp.name, errorField = F, cond = "FieldFilled(F) And [F] != <op>")` and `validateWith` evaluates it after the explicit rules. `ComputationDef` gains a `name`; `InterpreterModelLoader` reads the computation element's name. **Scope (superseded 2026-07-07 by IF65):** this increment synthesized only the **single-unconditional-alternative** form; the guarded/tiered generated condition (the kernel's `ComputationAlternativesJoiner` join) has since been implemented — IF65 carries the full mechanism. Two sub-fixes the date-range computed field surfaced: `eq` now compares `Val.Range` by endpoint equality, and `neFillable` falls back to symmetric fillability for unordered (non-number/text) operands instead of calling `vglCompare`. *Locked:* `adapter.laws.ComputationValidationDiffTest` — a disagreeing vs agreeing stored `CoverageWindow` (a `DateRange` computation authored via the typed `Computation` builder), both vs the real kernel.

### IF33 — a computed DATE is stored in the TARGET field's format, not ISO (FIXED 2026-06-26, graduated from IG19)

The kernel stores a computed date in the **target field's declared format** (KERNEL-FINDINGS §3 "a DATE value is stored and formal-checked in the field's DECLARED format"): `AddYears(OrderDate, 2)` into a `dd.MM.yyyy` field yields `30.06.2026`. The interpreter's `toComputed` emitted the raw interpreter-ISO `2026-06-30` for any `Val.Txt` — a divergence on `compute()` for a date computed into a non-ISO target. (The number-scale half of the original IG19 candidate looked like parity — `ComputeDiffTest`'s `49.90`→`49.9` on both — but that holds only for a `minFractionalDigits`-0 target: **refined by IF82**, a computed NUMBER stores PADDED to the target's `minFractionalDigits` — the "computed NUMBER stores PADDED" paragraph of KERNEL-FINDINGS §11 "compute()".)

The fix: `toComputed`'s `Val.Txt` branch routes through `renderScalarValue`, which reformats a computed value to the target's date format via `renderDateToFormat` when the target kind is `DATE` (STRING and DATE_TIME pass through). *Locked:* `adapter.laws.ComputeTargetFormatDiffTest` — the computed `Maturity` value vs the kernel.

**The DATE_TIME case needs no re-formatter — it is parity by construction (probe-resolved 2026-06-26).** A kernel `DATE_TIME` field admits only ONE date-bearing format, the ISO `yyyy-MM-dd'T'HH:mm:ss` (every other date-bearing datetime format is rejected `The date format is invalid`), and the degenerate time-only `HH:mm:ss` datetime can't receive a full `DateTime(...)` result (the implicit computation-validation compare is `MVK_INVALID_COMPARE_TO_DATE` at codegen). So every *authorable* computed-datetime target is ISO — exactly the interpreter's `Val.Txt` datetime output; a DATE_TIME re-formatter would be dead code. Kernel facts: KERNEL-FINDINGS §6 "DATE_TIME". *Locked:* `ComputeTargetFormatDiffTest.computedDateTimeUsesTargetFormatLikeKernel` (the ISO target — the only valid date-bearing one) + `cli.field.FieldVerbTest.dateAndTimeFieldFormatsAreFixedAndLocaleIndependent` (the allowlist).

### IF34 — an out-of-range computed value is bucketed ERRORED (FIXED 2026-06-26, graduated from IG13)

The kernel's `compute()` returns a computed value **but flags the instance** in `getComputedFieldInstancesWithErrors()` when the value is not valid for the target field — e.g. a number above its declared `maxValue` (probe-verified: `[A]*[B]` = 10000 into a `maxValue 999` field → `errored=true`, value `10000` still returned). `ComputeOutcome.ERRORED` had **zero producers** — the interpreter computed only VALUE/CLEARED, so a referencing consumer never saw the kernel's error bucket.

The fix: `toComputed` runs the SAME per-cell formal check the document applies (`formalError` with the target's kind/scale/format/constraints) on the rendered computed value; a formal error → `ComputeOutcome.ERRORED` (the value is still returned). In-range values stay VALUE; empty operands stay `0` (empty-as-0 is parity). *Locked:* `adapter.laws.ComputeErrorBucketDiffTest` — in-range VALUE vs out-of-range ERRORED, both vs the kernel's `errored` flag + value. **Residual (IG13, narrowed):** the kernel additionally **cascade-SKIPS** a computation that depends (directly/indirectly) on an errored computed field; the interpreter still overlays the errored value, so a *dependent* computation would diverge — tracked.

### IF35 — partial validation: a starred aggregate with a non-relevant cell is UNKNOWN (FIXED 2026-06-26, graduated from IG9)

Under a **subset** relevant set, the kernel treats a non-relevant field instance as UNKNOWN — and for a **starred aggregate** that means the whole aggregate is non-evaluable if ANY of its cells is non-relevant. Crucially, the kernel applies **NO directional 3VL** to the unknown cells (kernel-probed): `Max(Lines*/Qty) > 5` does **not** fire even when a *relevant* row alone (`10 > 5`) would force it — once a cell is unknown, `Max(10, U, U)` is Unknown, full stop. PV-1 proved the divergence (`Sum < 100`: kernel suppressed, interpreter fired — its aggregate/star resolution walked the document ignoring relevance). This was confirmed-real, not just untested.

The fix gates aggregate resolution by per-cell relevance: the `Interpreter` gains a nullable `cellRelevant: (path, reps) -> Boolean` (null in full validation — the gate, and its `starCells` call, is skipped entirely, so the flat-spike `RepDoc` path is untouched); in partial mode, `evalFunc` returns `Val.Unknown` for any function whose `Expr.Star` arg has a non-relevant cell (`ctx.starCells` supplies the per-cell reps). *Locked:* `adapter.laws.PartialValidationSubsetRepDiffTest` — the `Sum` subset case (formerly red) AND the `Max` non-directional case, both vs the real kernel's `validatePart`.

**Scope — superseded by IF42 (2026-06-26).** This blanket gate ("any non-relevant cell → UNKNOWN") is exact for the *simple* case it locks (one contiguous relevant row, the rest non-relevant) but NOT the general MIXED-relevance case the `PartialValidationFuzzDiffTest` later surfaced. **IF42** replaced it with the kernel's per-operator rule: all-rows aggregates (`Sum`/`Max`/…) need the starred level WILDCARDED (concrete enumeration → UNKNOWN), and `FirstFilled` is order-aware (first-filled-relevant). IF42 subsumes this finding's locked cases (row-1-only concrete → not wildcarded → still UNKNOWN). The partial **CustomCondition**-relevance / **global-field** items shipped as IF38/IF37.

### IF36 — `variantFilter: ONLY` elements are dropped at the interpreter's null variant (FIXED 2026-06-26, graduated from IG16)

A Field/Rule/Computation may carry a `variantFilter: { filterType: ONLY | ALL_EXCEPT, variants: [...] }` (KERNEL-FINDINGS "variantFilter"). The kernel's `VariantFiltering` omits the element from the built validation model when its filter rejects the active variant: with **variant == null** (the differential oracle's setting *and* dmtool's default), an `ONLY` element is **dropped**, an `ALL_EXCEPT` element is **kept**. The interpreter has no variant concept (always null), but `InterpreterModelLoader` ignored `variantFilter` entirely — so an `ONLY`-filtered rule was kept and could fire, diverging from the kernel.

The fix is at the loader (it never reaches the kernel-free engine): `acceptedByVariantFilter(typeSub)` returns false for `filterType == "ONLY"` (kept for `ALL_EXCEPT`/absent), and the Field/Rule/Computation cases skip a rejected element. *Locked:* `cli.interp.InterpreterRealModelDiffTest.variantFilterOnlyRuleIsDroppedLikeKernel` — a hand-authored `order-variant.dm.json` whose `DELIVERY_BEFORE_ORDER` rule carries `variantFilter: ONLY`; on a delivery-before-order instance the kernel fires nothing (rule dropped) and the interpreter now agrees. (modelkit/edit can't yet author `variantFilter` — `getVariantFilter` is a deferred authoring-parity slot — so the fixture is hand-written DM-JSON, verified kernel-valid by the differential.)

### IF37 — a GLOBAL field is auto-relevant in partial validation (FIXED 2026-06-26, graduated from IG11)

The kernel flags a field `global` (`IField.isGlobal`); in **partial** validation a global field is **always relevant**, regardless of the caller's relevant set. Source-confirmed it is the **md-runtime-service layer** — exactly the `IDocumentRtService.validatePart` boundary the differential oracle calls — that auto-adds them (`LowKernelApiUtils.addGlobalFieldsToRelevantEntities`, wildcarded at all reps), *not* the core validator; `IField.isGlobal`'s javadoc states the caller-responsibility contract (KERNEL-FINDINGS "Partial validation" → "GLOBAL fields"). The interpreter ignored the flag, so a rule whose error field is global was gated off under a relevant set that omitted it — diverging from the kernel (a confirmed `kernel=true interp=false`).

The fix: `FieldDef.global` + the derived `ModelDef.globalFields`, and `DmInterpreter.relevanceOf` makes a global field's instances relevant at any reps (`path in model.globalFields || …`) — full validation is untouched (it uses `ALL_RELEVANT`). The loader (`InterpreterModelLoader`) reads the Field-body `"global"` key, so a real loaded model carries the flag too. *Locked:* `adapter.laws.PartialValidationGlobalFieldDiffTest` (global fires, non-global control does not, both vs. the kernel) + `cli.interp.InterpreterRealModelDiffTest.loaderReadsTheGlobalFieldFlagIntoModelDef` (the loader mapping).

### IF38 — a CustomCondition receives the partial-validation relevant set, not null (FIXED 2026-06-26, graduated from IG10)

The kernel feeds `ICustomCondition.check` the relevant entities — **`null` for full validation, the relevant subset for partial** (source: `MainValidatorController.applikationsBedingung` reads `getAllPruefungsrelevanteEntitiesExtern()` only `isValidationPartWith3ValueLogic()`, else `null`). The set is the caller's *external* relevant entities (wildcards preserved, groups not expanded) **plus the auto-added globals** (IF37). A relevance-aware condition uses it to return false when its fields aren't relevant (the SPI contract). The interpreter's `CustomCondition.check` already had the `relevant` parameter but `Interpreter.evalCustom` hard-coded `relevant = null`, so a partial-mode condition behaved as if full — a confirmed `kernel=false interp=true` when a needed field was outside the relevant set.

The fix threads a materialized `Set<FieldPointer>?` from `DmInterpreter.validatePart` (via `customRelevantOf`: the caller's `RelevantEntity`s as `FieldPointer`s + each global as an all-reps-wildcard pointer; `null` in full) through `validateWith`/`messagesFor`/`rowOutcomes` into the `Interpreter`, which passes it to `check`. *Locked:* `adapter.laws.PartialValidationCustomConditionDiffTest` — a condition firing iff a second field (`Region`) is relevant: with only `CustomerName` relevant the kernel suppresses (Region not in the set) and the interpreter now matches; the control with `Region` relevant fires on both. (Harness: `RuntimeLaws.messagesPart(…, customConditions)`, the `validatePart` twin of `validateWithConditions`.)

### IF39 — partial uniqueness needs the duplicate PARTNER relevant too (FIXED 2026-06-26, graduated from the IG9-sibling probe)

Both uniqueness checks — the `uniqueIndex` auto-check and the `RepetitionNotUnique` rule — flag a row only if its duplicate **partner** is also relevant. Source: the kernel builds its duplicate caches from the **relevant fields only** (`ValidationCommand.befuelleCacheMitRelevantenFeldern`), so under a subset relevant set a row whose partner is not relevant has no duplicate to collide with. Probe-confirmed decisively: two rows share an index/key value, only row 1 relevant → **kernel flags 0, the interpreter flagged 1** (it detected duplicates by scanning ALL rows, then gated only emission). Same `kernel=0 interp=1` on both checks.

The fix is at the shared root — **duplicate DETECTION, not just emission, is relevance-filtered**: `duplicateIndexCells` skips non-relevant cells, and `repetitionNotUniqueMessages` filters participant rows by the error field's relevance *before* the duplicate grouping. Full validation is unchanged (`ALL_RELEVANT` → no filtering). *Locked:* `adapter.laws.PartialValidationUniqueIndexDiffTest` + `adapter.laws.PartialValidationRepetitionNotUniqueDiffTest` — each with a both-relevant control (flags both) and the row-1-only case (flags none, like the kernel).

### IF40 — compute(): a computation that reads an ERRORED dependency is SKIPPED (FIXED 2026-06-26, graduated from IG13)

In the computation **cascade**, when a dependency computes an **errored** value (out of range — IF34), the kernel does NOT compute any dependent that reads it: the dependent appears in **no** bucket of `compute()` — not changed, not cleared, not errored (probe-confirmed: `T := A` errors at `A=5000 > maxValue 999`, and `U := T + 1` is simply absent from the result, transitively `V := U + 1` too). The errored value is unusable, so the kernel skips the whole dependent subtree. The interpreter overlaid the errored value and computed the dependent (`U = 5001`) — a divergence.

The fix tracks **poisoned** `(target, reps)` instances in `compute()`: an ERRORED result poisons its target and is NOT overlaid (it is still reported as ERRORED); a computation whose computed dependencies include a poisoned instance is **skipped** (no outcome) and transitively poisons its own target. The empty-operand→0 and resolved-VALUE cascade paths are unchanged. *Locked:* `adapter.laws.ComputeCascadeErroredDiffTest` — the in-range control (full T/U/V cascade) and the errored case (kernel + interpreter both emit only the errored `T`, skipping `U`/`V`). KERNEL-FINDINGS "compute(): an errored dependency".

**Refined by IF85 (2026-07-08):** the "skip" is the READ-driven poison, not a static dependency pre-skip — a dependent that never actually reads the errored target (a short-circuit decided first) computes normally, and the kernel's "absent from every bucket" holds only for an input-EMPTY dependent (an input-FILLED one is reported **CLEARED**). The interpreter now poisons on the read and reports a poisoned instance CLEARED under its report-all contract (`kernelComputeGranularity` projects the unseeded ones for comparison).

### IF41 — `Dec` is a value type: scale-insensitive equality (FIXED 2026-06-26, graduated from IG4)

The interpreter's cross-platform decimal `Dec` (JVM `BigDecimal`, JS `big.js`) compared by **reference identity** — it declared `compareTo` but no `equals`/`hashCode`. Since `Val.Num(val v: Dec)` and `Expr.NumLit(val v: Dec)` are **data classes**, their auto-generated equality inherited that identity break: `Val.Num(dec("2.0")) != Val.Num(dec("2.0"))`, and a `Set<Val.Num>` never deduped. No *behavioral* path tripped it (every number comparison goes through `compareScaled19`/`compareTo`, never `equals` — which is why it stayed latent and was deferred), but it was a real landmine for any future AST dedup / caching / `Set` use.

The decision (the deferral's open question) is **scale-insensitive value equality**, consistent with how `Dec` is *compared* everywhere (`compareTo`, the kernel's `BigDecimal.compareTo`): `2.0 == 2.00`. Both actuals now implement `equals` via `compareTo(other) == 0` and `hashCode` off the canonical trailing-zero-stripped form (JVM `stripTrailingZeros().toPlainString()`, JS big.js `toString()`), each self-consistent. *Locked:* `interpreter/commonTest/DecEqualityTest` — equal-across-scales, distinct values, hashCode/`Set` dedup, and `Val.Num` equality, on JVM **and** JS.

### IF42 — partial validation: starred-aggregate relevance is PER-OPERATOR (FIXED 2026-06-26, graduated from IG20)

The partial-validation fuzz (`PartialValidationFuzzDiffTest`) surfaced that the blanket IF35 gate ("a starred aggregate with ANY non-relevant cell is UNKNOWN") was an oversimplification — the kernel's rule is per-operator, confirmed by a controlled probe (separate always-relevant error field, 2 rows, no fillable tail):

- **All-rows aggregates** (`Sum`, `MaxValue`, `MinValue`, `NumberOfDifferentValues`, `SumOfProducts`): the kernel evaluates them **only when the relevant set WILDCARDS the starred level** (`ALL_SEMANTIC` at the repeatable group, or a relevant ANCESTOR group whose descent makes all rows relevant). Enumerating concrete rows — **even ALL of them** — leaves the aggregate UNKNOWN: the `*` reference is "fully known" only via a wildcard (the BA's "encourages wildcarding every path level"; runtime: a `*`-read over a non-wildcarded set has unknown extent). The interpreter keyed on per-cell relevance, so concrete-both-rows wrongly evaluated → fired where the kernel suppressed.
- **`FirstFilled`**: **order-aware** — it returns the first FILLED cell's value; it is UNKNOWN only when a NON-relevant cell precedes that first filled cell (its filledness is unknown, so it can't be skipped). So it fires when the first filled row is relevant (wildcard / both-rows / that-row-only) and is UNKNOWN when that row is non-relevant. The interpreter's blanket gate over-suppressed it (any non-relevant cell → UNKNOWN), so the kernel fired where the interpreter didn't.

The fix splits the `evalFunc` partial gate: a `starRelevant: (starPath) -> Boolean` (threaded from `DmInterpreter.starRelevantOf` — does the relevant set wildcard every repeatable level of the path) gates the all-rows aggregates; `FirstFilled` is exempt and evaluated order-aware over `starCells` via `cellRelevant`. **Full validation is byte-identical** (`starRelevant`/`cellRelevant` null → the gate is skipped, `FirstFilled` falls to the plain first-filled path), so the `validateFull` perf path is untouched; the partial check is O(relevant-set), cheaper than the old per-cell scan. This subsumes IF35 (its row-1-only concrete cases → not wildcarded → still UNKNOWN). *Locked:* `adapter.laws.PartialValidationAggregateDiffTest` (curated per-operator cases) + the now-unrestricted `perf.PartialValidationFuzzDiffTest` (random document × relevant-subset). KERNEL-FINDINGS "Partial validation — starred-aggregate relevance".

### IF43 — baseline value checks: legal charset + leading/trailing blank (FIXED 2026-06-26, was the §3 "charset" out-of-scope item)

The §3 "string charset" item was recorded out-of-scope on a "not DM-JSON-declarable (no serialized key)" basis — flagged SUSPECT after the audit found the sibling `maxIntegerDigits` claim wrong (IF30), and indeed it WAS wrong. A probe proved the kernel runs three BASELINE value checks for **every** field kind (`FormalChecker.checkFieldValueFormat`), BEFORE the type-specific format check: **legal charset → leading blank → trailing blank**. They apply with no model configuration at all:
- **charset** → `ZeichenNichtImZeichensatz`: with no programming-language charset configured (a plain DM-JSON model), the default legal charset is the **BMP** (`0x0000-0xFFFF`, `CharsetUtil`: "empty list ≡ 0x0000-0xFFFF"), so any SUPPLEMENTARY-plane character — a UTF-16 surrogate, e.g. an emoji `😀` (U+1F600) — is illegal. BMP chars (`é`, `ü`, `€`, `™`, CJK) are legal.
- **leading blank** → `fuehrendesBlank`; **trailing blank** → `nachfolgendesBlank`. The baseline pre-empts the type check (a ` 2024-01-01` DATE is `fuehrendesBlank`, not a date-format error).

The interpreter missed all three for STRING, missed charset entirely, and mis-coded a NUMBER **trailing** blank as `fuehrendesBlank` (it had only a single `raw != raw.trim()` → `LEADING_BLANK` check). The fix is a single cross-kind `baselineValueError(raw, legalCharset)` (charset → leading → trailing) applied FIRST in `formalError` for every kind, replacing the number-only blank check. *Locked:* `adapter.laws.BaselineValueFormalDiffTest` — astral/leading/trailing across STRING/NUMBER/DATE + BMP-clean + internal-blank-clean controls, vs the kernel. KERNEL-FINDINGS §3 "baseline value checks".

**The charset is also CUSTOMIZABLE — the `LegalCharset` SPI (the customization-surface #1 goal).** The kernel's legal charset has two sources — a **project/codegen** config (the SPI's job here) and, **corrected by IF120**, the DM-JSON **`modelConfig.supportedCharacters`** model slot (which IS a serialized property, now read by `ModelLoader` and taking precedence over this SPI). This SPI is the consumer-supplied fallback for a project charset the DM-JSON does NOT carry: the default is the BMP, but a project can configure a narrower restricted set — the real case is **DIN 91379** (EU public-administration). To expose that customization axis the interpreter provides `DmInterpreter(legalCharset = …)`, default `LegalCharset.BMP`, plus `ofRanges(…)` for a custom singleton/range allow-list. A code point the configured set rejects fires `ZeichenNichtImZeichensatz`; the check is a cross-platform code-point walk (combines UTF-16 surrogate pairs, since `String.codePoints()` is JVM-only). *Locked:* `interpreter/commonTest/LegalCharsetTest` (BMP default + ASCII/range customizations, JVM+JS); the model-slot half is IF120. These locks prove singleton/range behavior, not the kernel's atomic combined-sequence form, which remains IG88. This is the first general instance of the CLAUDE.md "customization-surface parity" #1 goal (every kernel customization exposed on the API).

### IF44 — code-based `CustomFieldType` SPI: the imperative twin of `PredefinedType`, dispatched additively (FIXED 2026-06-27, graduated from IG21)

Custom field types were **declarative-only** — the registry value was `PredefinedType(minLength, maxLength, pattern)`, a *data* record interpreted by `accepts(value)` — so a consumer could declare a *format* constraint but not an *imperative* one (a checksum: IBAN mod-97, ISIN/Luhn). Custom *conditions* already had a code seam (`CustomCondition` is a `fun interface`); field types did not. IF44 adds the behaviour SPI `fun interface CustomFieldType { validate(value): Boolean }` — the kernel's `ICustomFieldType` client surface — and threads a consumer-supplied `customFieldTypes: Map<String, CustomFieldType>` through `DmInterpreter`/`Interpreter`.

**The dispatch is strictly ADDITIVE + monomorphic-preserving** (the [`INTERPRETER-PERFORMANCE.md`](INTERPRETER-PERFORMANCE.md) "hot path stays a monomorphic concrete call" rule): the per-cell custom-type check (`customTypeRejected`) and the `Valid`/`Invalid` predicate consult the declarative `predefinedTypes` registry **first** (the concrete, JIT-inlined `accepts`), and the polymorphic `CustomFieldType.validate` only when that misses — so a consumer who registers no code-based type pays nothing, and the declarative path is byte-for-byte unchanged. **Locked** by `CustomFieldTypeSpiTest` (commonTest, JVM+JS — code-type accept/reject + declarative-and-code coexistence) and the perf gate `CustomTypePerfTest`; the kernel differentials `PredefinedTypeValidityDiffTest` + `CustomFieldTypeBoundsDiffTest` stay green. **Graceful degrade:** a *registered* code-based type whose `validate()` throws (e.g. dmtool's Node-worker bridge when `node` is down) is caught in `formalErrors` and the cell is reported `unsupported` per cell, not crashed. This is the interpreter half of the CLI's `--custom-field-types-js` (FEATURE-GAPS G31, the JS escape); a library consumer registers an in-process `CustomFieldType` directly. Part of the CLAUDE.md "customization-surface parity" #1 goal.

### IF45 — `applyComputations`: the apply-computed-back primitive + the compute-then-validate flow (2026-06-28)

The kernel keeps validation and computation **separate** — `validateFull` does NOT compute first (KERNEL-FINDINGS "validateFull does NOT run computations") — and the consumer applies computed values back with `IDocumentComputationResult.applyTo(doc)` before re-validating (the **form-engine flow** `compute → apply → validate`). The interpreter exposed the two halves (`validate`, `compute`) but **not** the apply-back step, so a consumer couldn't reproduce the realistic "is this document valid in the app?" answer — a required-or-referenced **computed** field validated raw false-fires `mandatoryField` / reads empty.

IF45 adds the clean-room twin of `applyTo`: **`DmInterpreter.applyComputations(doc): Document`** returns the updated document — a VALUE outcome **sets** the computed cell, an ERRORED or CLEARED outcome **empties** it (matching `applyTo`, which applies `WithChanges` and clears both `WithErrors` and `Cleared`; KERNEL-FINDINGS). It rides the existing reps-aware compute core (`computeInstances`, the cascade/poison machinery) and materializes the result through a new `Document.withComputed(overrides)` clone (placements re-added through the public builders so the index/auto-grouping/document-order invariants hold). The form-engine flow is then just `validate(applyComputations(doc))`.

**Faithfulness** is locked by the differential `adapter.laws.ComputeApplyDiffTest` — for a required-computed field and a rule over a computed field, the interpreter's `applyComputations` + `validate` equals the kernel's `compute().applyTo().validateFull()` in BOTH modes (raw false-fires / applied clears), and the two modes genuinely differ. The **engine-level** `validate == validateFull` parity stays foundational (every other differential rests on it); the composed path is verified on top, never replacing it. Consumers: the CLI `model eval` / `rule eval` run the composed flow (`--no-computations` opts to raw); the kernel side (the differential oracle) is `KernelAdapter.evaluateApplied`. Part of the CLAUDE.md "customization-surface parity / match-the-kernel" goal — the apply-back primitive was a missing surface, not just a CLI convenience.

### IF46 — `NumberOfFilledGroups(/Group*)` counts a repeatable group's INSTANTIATED repetitions (FIXED 2026-07-06, graduated from IG26)

`NumberOfFilledGroups` has two rendered shapes. The **plain multi-group** form (`NumberOfFilledGroups(g1, g2)`, ≥2 distinct groups) counts each listed group that is filled — it worked, its operands are plain `Expr.Field`. The **starred single-repeatable-group** form — `NumberOfFilledGroups(/Group*)`, which the `RootGroupCount` DSL node (a repeatable *root* group) and `Dsl.numberOfFilledGroups(repeatableGroup)` (a repeatable non-root group) both render — carries a bare `Expr.Star` operand, and the count branch did `pathOf(it)` (`it as Expr.Field`) → an **uncaught `ClassCastException` that aborted the whole `validate()` run** (root mechanism R1, the `pathOf`-assumes-a-plain-field class; sibling of the `$`/star gaps). This was authored/rendered/kernel-accepted (`RootGroupCountLawsTest`) but never fed to `DmInterpreter` — only render-checked (`RootGroupCountReadTest`) or kernel-only.

**The kernel semantics (probed 2026-07-06):** the count is the group's **instantiated repetition count** — a created-but-empty row **counts** (row1-filled + row2-empty-instantiated → 2; a lone empty row → 1; no rows → 0). This is exactly the repeatable-group `GroupFilled` notion (a repeatable group is "filled" once its row is instantiated — KERNEL-FINDINGS §4/§9), so no new "≥1 field filled" per-row check is needed. The fix dispatches a single-`Star` operand to `ctxOf(star).rowContexts(star.path).size` — **O(rows)**, reusing the already-scoped + cached row-context list (the same `rowContexts` the `$` filtered quantifier uses); the plain form keeps its `groupFilled` count. *Locked:* `adapter.laws.NumberOfFilledGroupsStarDiffTest` (filled / empty-instantiated / mixed / absent rows, `== / > / >=` against the kernel). Surfaced by the 2026-07-06 renderer-vocabulary audit (IG26); the durable renderer-vocabulary round-trip guard (so this class can't recur) is the standing prevention.

### IF47 — `$` outer-iteration correlation: the enclosing-row seam + the O(N) equality hash-join (FIXED 2026-07-06, graduated from IG23)

A `$`-marked reference inside a `Having` filter correlates the inner filtered row to the **enclosing (outer) iteration row** — the kernel BA's "predecessors" pattern `AtLeastOneFieldFilled(../G* / F Having CurrentRepetition(/G) != CurrentRepetition($/G) And [F] == [$F])` (KERNEL-FINDINGS §"$ correlation is implemented, both directions"). dmkits reads/authors/renders it in full, but the interpreter **hard-failed at parse** (`parser: unexpected '$'`), making `model eval` / `rule eval` / `model compute` unusable on any model that uses it (reported on a real corpus model). It hid because the interpreter's parity is only as complete as the differential + fuzz *feed* it, and `$` was never fed to `DmInterpreter` — the process root cause the durable guard (below) closes.

The three-layer fix: **(1) parser** accepts `$` in all four atom positions (`[$path]`, `CurrentRepetition($path)`, bare `$path`, value-list `$Group*/field`) → an `outer` flag on `Expr.Field`/`Expr.Star`/`Expr.EnumCategory`; **(2)** the evaluator threads the enclosing iteration as `outer: EvalContext`, set only while a `Having` sub-interpreter (`havingInterp`) evaluates, so `ctxOf` routes a `$`-ref to `outer` and a bare `$` at top level is a fail-fast error; **(3)** a filtered quantifier over a star (`starQuantTally`) iterates the group's rows and keeps those the `Having` accepts. Bit-exact via `adapter.laws.OuterCorrelationDiffTest` (self-excluded / no-self-exclusion / non-equality-fallback / empty-and-zero-join-keys) — the very differential whose absence hid the gap. Clean-room from the BA example, no kernel delegation.

**Performance — an O(N) hash-join, faster than the kernel (INTERPRETER-PERFORMANCE.md).** A naive filtered self-correlation is O(N²) (each of N outer rows scans N inner rows). The equality self-correlation shape `[X] == [$X]` (± a `CurrentRepetition` self-exclusion) is recognized (`parseCorrelationJoin`) and resolved by a value-keyed **bucket map** the `Document` builds + caches once per (group, scope) — O(N) — then an O(1) lookup per outer row (`hashJoinTally`, self-subtracting the outer row's own contribution when self-excluded). This is the kernel's own linear strategy; a non-equality `Having` keeps the O(N²) scan fallback (still `$`-correct). Gated by `adapter.perf.OuterCorrelationPerfTest` (sub-cubic per-row + an against-kernel ratio via `RuntimeLaws.kernelEngine`) — measured **8–11× faster than the kernel** on the self-correlation curve. **Known edge (shared with the plain star path):** a malformed inner field inside a `$`-`Having` uses the path-only `invalid` check (both fast-path and fallback), so per-cell malformed-in-`Having` is not yet differential-covered — closes when the fuzz generates `$` + malformed cells (the durable guard).

### IF48 — string concatenation: `+` is kind-dispatched, not numeric-only (FIXED 2026-07-06, graduated from IG24)

`+` was numeric-only (`evalArith` → `num`), so a `Concatenation` computation with two present STRING operands hit `num(Val.Txt)` → an **uncaught `IllegalStateException` that aborted the whole `compute()` run** (root mechanism R2) — highest real-world impact, since `FullName = FirstName + " " + LastName` is one of the commonest computations and it broke the moment the fields were filled. It was authored/rendered/kernel-accepted but only render-checked (`ConcatenationReadTest`), never fed to `DmInterpreter`.

The fix kind-dispatches `+`: when either operand is textual (a `Val.Txt`, a STRING-field ref, or a nested `+`-concatenation) the result is **string concatenation**, else numeric addition — matching the kernel's overloaded `+`. An **empty** string operand contributes `""` (kernel-probed: `FirstName + " " + LastName` with `LastName` empty → `"Jane "`, not Unknown), so `concatText` maps a `Val.Txt` to its text, a `Val.Num` to its display form, and empty/other to `""`. *Locked:* `adapter.laws.ConcatComputeDiffTest` (both filled / first-empty / last-empty against the kernel's `compute()`).

### IF49 — group-scoped field-fill quantifiers: expand to descendant fields, over the kernel's TWO iteration ranges (FIXED 2026-07-06, graduated from IG25)

A field-fill quantifier can take a **group scope** rather than a field list — `AllFieldsFilled(/Order/ShippingAddress)`, `AtLeastOneFieldFilled(/Order/Items*)`. Two failures: a **bare group** operand was treated as ONE field (`ctx.filled("/Order/ShippingAddress")` on a group path → **quiet wrong verdict**, the worst kind), and a **starred group** operand crashed (`pathOf(Star)` → `ClassCastException`, aborting the run — root mechanism R1). It was authored/rendered/kernel-accepted (`GroupScopedFieldFillReadTest`) but never fed to `DmInterpreter`. The **same class latently mis-evaluated `AllFieldsFilled` over a single *starred field*** (`AllFieldsFilled(/Items*/Sku)`) — the old `starQuantTally` flattened instantiated cells, which is the wrong range (see below).

**The kernel semantics (source `RuntimeController` + `ExpandService`, probe-confirmed — KERNEL-FINDINGS §1).** A group scope means "the fields inside the group", **recursively** (the kernel `ExpandService` → `getAllContainedFields`, so a nested subgroup's fields count). The runtime then uses **two iteration ranges** for a starred entity: `AllFieldsFilled`/`NotAllFieldsFilled` iterate the **full DECLARED** repetition range (an un-instantiated rep reads empty → `AllFieldsFilled(/Lines*)` is TRUE only when *every declared rep* is filled), while `AtLeastOne`/`NoField`/`MoreThanOne`/`NotExactlyOne` clamp to the **instantiated** rows; `FieldsNotCollectivelyFilled` mixes (instantiated-≥1-filled AND declared-≥1-empty). A bare non-repeatable group is one declared rep, so the ranges coincide.

The fix: `EvalContext.fillScope(scopePath, isGroup)` (the general `Document` resolves it) enumerates the descendant fields from the model (`kinds` under the scope) and returns a `FillScope` carrying **both** ranges — the instantiated 3-state tally (`tInst`/`fInst`/`nInst`, malformed cell → `hasUnknownInst`, exact per-cell via `resolveValue`) **and** the declared flags (`allFilledDeclared`/`anyEmptyDeclared`, a short-circuiting scan over `1..declaredMax` at each repeatable level — **O(instantiated + 1)** in the common under-filled case, O(data) when fully filled, never worse). `Interpreter` routes a fill quantifier with a scope operand (a bare-group `Field`, or a `Star` **without** a `Having` — a `Star` *with* a `Having` stays the IF47 filtered-correlation path) through `fillScopeFires`/`fillScopeType`; a plain field list keeps the existing `quantCounts` path (byte-identical), and the instantiated-range ops reduce to the same `quantifierFires`. *Locked:* `adapter.laws.GroupScopedFieldFillDiffTest` (bare / starred / nested-recursion × both ranges × firing **and** polarity, against the kernel). Surfaced by the 2026-07-06 renderer-vocabulary audit (IG25).

### IF50 — entity-valued value list: a starred value ref is the flattened FILLED cell set (FIXED 2026-07-06, graduated from IG27)

A value-list quantifier can take an **entity** value list — `AtLeastOneFieldValueIncludedInValueList(f In /Order/Items*/Sku)` — where the value side is a **starred repetition list** (a field's cells across all rows), not literal constants. `evalValueListQuant` mapped `evalExpr` over the value refs, so an `Expr.Star` value hit "a starred path must sit inside an aggregate" (root mechanism R1's value-list sibling). Authored/rendered/kernel-accepted but only the LITERAL form was fed to `DmInterpreter` (`ValueListQuantifierDiffTest`).

**The kernel semantics (probed 2026-07-06):** the value SET is the starred field's **filled** cell values across all rows — empties dropped (`PC=A In Sku=[A,B]` → in; `In []` → not in; an empty field on the left is skipped). The fix expands a `Star` value ref via `ctxOf(v).star(v.path)` (the filled values; the initial "a malformed cell → the whole quantifier `Unknown`" treatment was **refined by IF60** — malformed handling is per-operator asymmetric, KERNEL-FINDINGS §8 "Runtime semantics of the shape-B QUANTIFIERS"), leaving a literal or plain-field value ref on the existing `evalExpr` path. *Locked:* `adapter.laws.EntityValueListDiffTest` (`AtLeastOne`/`No`/`NotAll` membership, empty value set, empty field). Surfaced by the 2026-07-06 renderer-vocabulary audit (IG27).

### IF51 — the multi-operand aggregate forms: multi-field / group / typed, not single-star-only (FIXED 2026-07-06, graduated from IG29–IG32)

The value/count aggregate family (`Sum`/`MaxValue`/`MinValue`/`NumberOfDifferentValues`/`NumberOfFilledFields`) was modeled **single-starred-operand only** — `starVals` did `f.args.single() as Expr.Star`, `NumberOfFilledFields` did `pathOf(field)`, and `Aggregates.numberOfDifferentValues` folded via `numOf` (numeric). But rulekit + the kernel also accept the explicit **multi-field list** (`Sum(A, B)`), a **GROUP** operand (`NumberOfFilledFields(/Addr)`), and **non-numeric** (STRING/ENUM) element types. Each form is authored/rendered/kernel-accepted (`MultiFieldSumReadTest`) but had **no** `*DiffTest` feeding it to `DmInterpreter` — the exact renderer-vocabulary blind spot, the second sweep's `feldListenOp` sibling of IF46–IF50. Four forms crashed **uncaught** (`IllegalArgumentException`/`ClassCastException`, aborting the whole `validate()`/`compute()` run — root mechanism R1: an operand that is not a single plain field/star); one silently miscounted.

The fixes, all clean-room + differential-locked:
- **multi-field value aggregates** (`Sum`/`MaxValue`/`MinValue`) — `starVals` now flattens across **all** operands (`aggOperandVals`): a `Star` expands over its rows (a `Having` filters them), a plain field contributes its value when filled. An empty operand is **dropped** (not folded as 0), so `MinValue(20, empty) == 20`; an all-empty set folds to the kernel's `0` identity (KERNEL-FINDINGS §1 supplement).
- **`NumberOfDifferentValues` typed** — `Aggregates.numberOfDifferentValues` counts distinct by a canonical **TYPED** key (`"N"+scale19` / `"S"+str` / `"B"+bool`, mirroring `fieldValuesNotUnique`), so a STRING/ENUM operand no longer hits `numOf`'s numeric `error`.
- **`NumberOfFilledFields` scope** — `filledFieldsValue` counts across operands: a plain field 1-when-filled, a **starred** repetition list its filled cells across rows (IG31, the `pathOf(Star)` crash), a **group** operand its filled descendant fields recursively (`fillScope(path, isGroup=true)` — IG32, the "group counted as one unfilled field" silent-wrong). A present-but-**malformed** cell is a formal error that makes the whole count **non-evaluable → `Val.Unknown`** (the rule is suppressed, exactly as `combine` does for the value aggregates) — **not** "count the valid ones and skip it." The **fuzz caught this**: my first cut counted present cells, so a doc with 4 valid + 1 malformed `Qty` fired `NumberOfFilledFields(Lines* Qty) >= 2` in the interpreter while the kernel suppressed it (the malformed field is a formal error the referencing rule inherits). Locked deterministically by the malformed case in `MultiFieldAggregateDiffTest.numberOfFilledFieldsStar` + the differential fuzz's `NF` rule.
- **count polarity** — the count family now types directionally (grow-only, KERNEL-FINDINGS §1 supplement): `funcFill` returns `Fill.of(canGrow = leafOmission, canShrink = false)`, so a fired `count >= n` is VALUE (was wrongly OMISSION under the old symmetric fill).

**IG33 — `SumOfProducts` over plain fields — is parity-by-construction, NOT a gap:** the kernel rejects it at codegen with `MVK_REPEATABLE_LEVEL_REQUIRED` ("All fields must be repeatable at least at one layer"), so a plain-field `SumOfProducts` never appears in a valid/loadable model and the interpreter's `sumOfProducts` (which resolves both operands as stars) is unreachable on any valid input (INTERPRETER-GAPS §3). *Locked:* `adapter.laws.MultiFieldAggregateDiffTest` — every form × {number, string} × firing **and** polarity, against the kernel; it is the durable net for this sub-class, keyed on the DSL's *emit* shapes (multi-field / group / typed) the operator-keyed guards can't see. Surfaced by the 2026-07-06 second interpreter-vocabulary sweep (IG29–IG32).

### IF52 — the omitted-year date literal `"13.07."` injects the model's base year (FIXED 2026-07-06, graduated from IG22)

A date literal can omit the year — `"13.07."` (day.month., trailing dot) — a **reference-year** feature: the kernel injects the model's `baseYear`, so `[OrderDate] > "13.07."` is a full-date comparison against `13.07.<baseYear>`. The interpreter's `coerceDate` canonicalized only the full `dd.MM.yyyy` form, so the omitted-year literal stayed an un-coerced string and the comparison diverged.

**The kernel semantics (probed 2026-07-06 — this CORRECTS the IG22 hypothesis).** The injected year is exactly `baseYear`, **not a floor**: on the order fixture (`baseYear = 2020`), `[OrderDate] > "13.07."` fires for `2024-07-20` and `2020-07-20` but **not** for `2019-07-20` (a floor would fire) nor `2020-07-10` (day/month within the base year). And a model with **no** base year **rejects** the literal at validation (`MVK_INVALID_COMPARE_TO_DATE`, "only allowed to compare dates with other fitting dates") — so the interpreter only ever sees `"13.07."` when a base year is present; there is no floor case to handle. The fix teaches `coerceDate` to match the `dd.MM.` shape and append `baseYear` (zero-padded to 4 digits) before the ISO parse; the full `dd.MM.yyyy` path is unchanged, and a non-date string still passes through untouched. *Locked:* `adapter.laws.OmittedYearDateDiffTest` (`>` and `<` × the base-year boundary dates, against the kernel). KERNEL-FINDINGS §6 supplement.

### IF53 — full validation: an INSTANTIATED repeatable-group row is *content* (evaluated), not just a valued field (FIXED 2026-07-06, graduated from IG34)

The interpreter's row-content gate — `EvalContext.evaluated` (built by `Document.attachByDepth`), which feeds the `Interpreter.gated` rule-eval check — counted only **valued fields**. So a rule whose iteration scope contained an all-field-empty but **instantiated** repeatable-group row was skipped, where the kernel **evaluates** it: the kernel treats *creating a repeatable row as content in itself* (KERNEL-FINDINGS §4/§9's `GroupFillCache` split — a repeatable group is "filled" once its row exists). Two shapes diverged, both firing OMISSION in the kernel and nothing in the interpreter:

- a **per-row comparison** `[Qty] < 5` over instantiated-empty `Lines` rows (empty `Qty` = 0 < 5) — fired on **every** instantiated row;
- an **ancestor-scoped empty-identity aggregate** `MinValue` over `Lines* then Qty`, `< 5` (empty identity 0 < 5) — the instantiated `Lines` rows make the non-repeatable `/Cart` scope content-bearing, so it fires @ the first row.

**Fix (single-sourced — the "content" notion is one thing, two consumers).** The `Document.Index` gains a `content` list = **a valued field OR an instantiated REPEATABLE-group row** (a non-repeatable group's mere structural presence is excluded — its group placement has `value == null` and is not in `declaredReps`). Both consumers route through it: **`attachByDepth`** (the rule-eval `evaluated` gate) and **`nonRepGroupFilledDepths`** (the `GroupFilled` OPERATOR for a non-repeatable group). The second is the **sibling the root-cause sweep found** (both once scanned valued fields only): `GroupNotFilled(Box)` over a non-repeatable `Box` with 3 instantiated-empty repeatable `Items` rows — the kernel reports `Box` **filled** (the instantiated rows are content), the interpreter wrongly reported it unfilled → `GroupNotFilled` mis-fired. So the "non-repeatable group is filled iff a descendant FIELD is filled" reading of KERNEL-FINDINGS §4/§9 was incomplete: an instantiated repeatable descendant row also fills it. The truly-empty non-repeatable `/Order` of [[IF6]]/`EmptyRowGateDiffTest` is unchanged (no instantiated repeatable descendant, no valued field). The `evaluated` flag stays O(1) per row (precomputed, memoized per group); `content` is O(document), same order as the valued-field scan it replaces — no new N² term (perf-gate confirmed). **Zero rows → still no fire** (no content). Surfaced by the aggregate-fuzz's re-added `MinValue < 5` rule (the only unguarded empty-firing aggregate in the net). *Locked:* `adapter.laws.RepeatableRowContentDiffTest` (rule-eval gate) + `adapter.laws.NonRepGroupFilledContentDiffTest` (the `GroupFilled` sibling) + the full differential fuzz. KERNEL-FINDINGS §2 + §4/§9 supplements.

### IF54 — partial validation: a RELEVANT instance is evaluated regardless of content — empty-as-0 / empty-identity FIRE (FIXED 2026-07-06, graduated from IG34)

The other half of [[IF53]], in **partial** validation: the content gate does **not** apply at all — the kernel checks **every RELEVANT error-field instance**, firing a comparison's empty-as-0 or an empty-identity aggregate even on a **non-content-bearing (empty / phantom)** relevant row. This is a genuine **full-vs-partial asymmetry** — relevance is the kernel's explicit "check this," which `validateFull` has no analogue of. Probed 2026-07-06:

- `MinValue` over `Lines* then Qty`, `< 5` on a `/Cart`-relevant **empty document** (zero `Lines` rows) → the kernel FIRES `OMISSION @ /Cart[1]/Lines[1]/Qty` — a **phantom** row that does not exist — though `validateFull` over 0 rows does **not** fire ([[IF53]]);
- `[Note] < 5` on a **relevant empty** `Note` → FIRES (empty-as-0), though full validation of a blank field does not ([[IF6]]);
- the **IF42 star-relevance gate still bounds an aggregate under a *concrete* relevant row**: `MinValue` with only a concrete `Lines[1]` relevant → the star is not wildcard-relevant → UNKNOWN → no fire (matching the kernel).

**Fix:** `Interpreter.gated` is bypassed when `partial` (the caller supplied a relevant set → `cellRelevant` is non-null) — the upstream `DmInterpreter.rowOutcomes` relevance gate has already decided (it only reaches an eval for a relevant error-field instance), so re-applying the content gate would wrongly suppress. Full validation (`cellRelevant == null`) keeps the gate ([[IF53]]). *Locked:* `adapter.laws.RepeatableRowContentDiffTest.partialRelevantEmptyInstanceIsEvaluated` + `perf.PartialValidationFuzzDiffTest` (the `MinValue < 5` rule re-added). KERNEL-FINDINGS §2 supplement. IG34 is now closed — the interpreter gap ledger's §1 is empty.

### IF55 — the hand-written parser accepts the legal stored DSL: case variants, `;;` comments, `..Name`, `For` on bare args (FIXED 2026-07-06, graduated from IG35)

The interpreter's parser (`parse/Parser.kt`) is the **default `model eval`/`rule eval` path** and must accept whatever the real kernel STORES — but a Phase-0 sweep (SEMANTICS-FORMALIZATION-PROPOSAL S1) found it rejected several legal shapes real conditions use. WI-P completed it, TDD-driven by a recreated sweep (`adapter.laws.ParserCorpusSweepTest`) — which is now a **permanent guard** with two postures: the catalog + fuzz DSL parse **100%** (always-on, in-repo, must stay green) and the `a12-kernel-mm` metamodels + A12 Tools workspace corpora parse **100%** (opt-in, external-sibling, `assumeTrue`-skip). Before/after: mm conditions 647→657/657; workspace conditions 177→368/368, operations 206→234/234. The shapes:

- **Keyword case variants** (the kernel fact is homed: KERNEL-FINDINGS "Condition lexing") — `And`/`and`/`AND`, `Or`/`or`/`OR`, `In`/`in`/`IN`, EXACTLY as `LexerTerminals_en` enumerates them (the UND/ODER/IN token families) — a fixed `KEYWORD_VARIANTS` table in `matchKeyword`, NOT blanket case-folding, so `aNd`/`oR` stay rejected. The `to`/`To`/`TO` (ZU) family is intentionally absent: its only user is the `ValueNotConsistent` separator, which the interpreter does not parse (not in the catalog, not in the corpora) — no match site, so adding it would be dead.
- **`;;` comments** — already skipped in `skipWs` (kernel `Comment` token), incl. the **multi-line continuation** form; `;;` inside a string literal stays data (`readString` doesn't skip). The kernel channels `Comment` as `before`/`after` a (sub)condition/operation; skipping it wherever whitespace is skipped is a safe superset for *reading* stored conditions.
- **`..Name` turning-point paths** (the kernel fact is homed: KERNEL-FINDINGS §10 "relative path forms") — the grammar's `relativeUp` (`relUp? '..' idName?`). Clean-room from `CheckRelativeUpImpl`: the up-count is set purely by the number of `..` tokens; `Name` merely NAMES the ancestor landed on (the kernel raises `MVK_INVALID_ENTITY` on mismatch) and NEVER changes the target, so `..Name/rest` ≡ `../rest` and `..Name` ≡ the ancestor group. **Behavior-bearing → locked against the real kernel** (`adapter.laws.NamedAncestorPathDiffTest`: kernel accepts `..Name`, interpreter agrees, and it fires bit-identically to its plain-`..` twin), plus the syntactic `interpreter.NamedAncestorPathParseTest`.
- **`For` semantic-index suffix on BARE args** — `Fuer` rides the kernel's `entitySpec`, so it can follow any entity reference, not only inside `[...]`: `FieldValueAsString(field For Index)` and semantic-index refs inside a `+` string concatenation now parse to the same `Expr.SemanticIndex` the bracketed form does (a shared `entitySuffix` helper) — eval already locked by `SemanticIndexDiffTest`.
- **Value-list literal form** — `FieldValueIncludedInValueList(field, "a", "b", …)` (the kernel's comma-separated `wertListenSpezifier2`) already mapped onto the generic `Cond.Pred` the evaluator's `valueInList` interprets (`ValueListDiffTest`); WI-P pins the parse (`interpreter.ValueListLiteralParseTest`).

Parser-source decision (INTERPRETER-SPEC §11, fork F4): the gap was small and enumerable, so **complete the hand-written recursive-descent parser** rather than adopt the kernel parse-tree-api or generate ANTLR from the EUPL `.g4` — keeping the component kernel-free (native-safe / JS-compilable) and the `.g4`-derivative licensing question moot (reading the grammar for syntax *shapes* is mechanism, not expression). No GRAMMAR-LEDGER row classification flips (that ledger tracks the rulekit renderer/adapter, not the interpreter parser).

### IF56 — the loader carries a `TimeType.format`, completing the date-ish format quartet (FIXED 2026-07-06, graduated from IG36 — TimeType half)

`ModelLoader.formatOf` read a declared rendering format for DATE / DATE_TIME / DATE_RANGE but **dropped TIME** — an asymmetry, since the interpreter's format-driven `parseTimeToIso` (and `ModelDef.timeFormats`) is built to receive it. Now it reads `TimeType.format` too, so a `TIME` field carries its declared format instead of silently falling back to `HH:mm:ss`.

This is locked as a **loader unit test** (`interpreter/…/load/ModelLoaderTest.carriesNonDefaultTimeFormat`), **not a kernel differential** — because the kernel itself makes it unobservable: kernel **codegen rejects a non-default `TimeType` format** ("Field … : The date format is invalid" — probed `HH:mm`, `HH.mm.ss`, `H:mm:ss`, all rejected; the A12 Tools workspaces use only the default `HH:mm:ss`), so you cannot build a kernel-valid model with a non-default time format to differentially test against. The fix therefore matters only for the **standalone interpreter**, where a hand-built model may declare another time format and the interpreter should honor it — pure loader completeness, no eval-path change (an existing `HH:mm:ss` field's `ModelDef.timeFormats` value is unchanged; only `FieldDef.format` goes from `null` to the explicit format). The sibling **`DateFragmentType` half** of the packed IG36 shipped separately as IF57 (below).

### IF57 — `DateFragmentType` is a DATE completed to its earliest concrete date (FIXED 2026-07-07, graduated from IG36 — fragment half)

A `DateFragmentType` field stores a date **fragment** in its `getFormatOfFragment` — one of `yyyy-MM`, `yyyy`, `MM`, `MM-dd` (a proper subset of the date components). The kernel **collapses it to a plain `Date`** (KERNEL-FINDINGS §6 "a date fragment IS a `DateOperand`"): when it reads a fragment field for a comparison / `Add*` / extraction, it **completes the missing components to the *earliest* concrete date** — its `feldWertAlsDatum` INTERVAL_START rule: missing **month → `01`**, missing **day → `01`**, missing **year → the model base year** (`modelInfo.baseYear`, kernel default 2000). Then everything runs through the ordinary date machinery. Both kernel strategies share that one runtime completion (`RuntimeController.feldWertAlsDatum`; the codegen's `FieldIdOperation` emits `controller.feldWertAlsDatum(...)` for a date field), so the interpreter matches both — kernel-fact + locks in KERNEL-FINDINGS §6 "date-fragment runtime completion".

**Why this was not a loader tweak (the IG36 trap).** Mapping a fragment to `Kind.DATE` + carrying the format, then parsing it as a *full* date, mis-evaluates a month-/year-only fragment (a full-date parse of `2024-06` or `2024` fails outright). The fix is an **eval** effort with a precision model:

- **Precision model** — `DatePrecision` gains a `FRAGMENT` case alongside `FULL`/`DAY_OPTIONAL` (the kernel's shared "teilbekannt" axis). *Which* components are present is carried by the field's format string itself (the kernel's `DateFormat` component partition), so this one marker + the format drives the completion — robust to any of the four fragment formats without a four-way enum.
- **Loader** — `ModelLoader` maps `DateFragmentType` → `Kind.DATE`, `format` = `formatOfFragment`, `datePrecision` = `FRAGMENT` (so the field lands in `ModelDef.dateFormats`/`datePrecisions` like any date).
- **Eval** — `DateMath.completeFragmentToIso(raw, format, baseYear)` completes a fragment value to its earliest ISO date (returning null on a bad month / impossible day → `datumFormatFalsch`); `Document.resolveValue`'s `FRAGMENT` branch canonicalizes to it (threading the model's `baseYear`), and `formalError`'s `FRAGMENT` branch well-forms it. After that, comparison / `coerceDate` / `dateShift` / `dateComponent` / `producesDate` all work **unchanged** — a fragment is just a completed ISO `Val.Txt`.
- **Base year is observable** — with a declared base year, a year-less fragment (`MM`/`MM-dd`) completes its year to *that* year and so becomes comparable to a year-bearing full date (the partition's base-year relaxation), which is why `baseYear` is threaded into `Document`, not hard-coded 2000.
- **Seeding is free** — `ValidValues`'s `Kind.DATE -> renderByFormat(field.format, …)` renders a valid fragment value (`2024-06`, `06-15`, …) once the loader carries the fragment format; no seeding change.
- **The interpreter does NOT re-enforce the authoring gates.** The comparability partition (`MVK_INVALID_COMPARE_TO_DATE` — both operands carry a year or neither, unless a base year relaxes it) and the `Add*`/extract granularity gating (`MVK_WRONG_DATE_FORMAT_FOR_OP` — `AddMonths` needs a month, `AddYears` a year, `AddDays`/`DayFromDate` a day) are **kernel authoring-time (codegen) rejections**, so a valid/loadable model contains only legal combos; the interpreter evaluates only those (matches rulekit's read behavior; the kernel gates are homed at KERNEL-FINDINGS §6 "date-fragment comparability partition" + "partially-known-date extraction gating").

**Locks:** `adapter.laws.DateFragmentComparisonDiffTest` — the kernel differential over own-domain `membership.dm.json` (no base year) + `membership-baseyear.dm.json` (base 2024), loaded into BOTH engines via `ModelLoader` (so it locks the loader case + the eval together): the completion for all four formats, `yyyy-MM`/`yyyy` vs a full date, `MM`/`MM-dd` year-less comparisons, `Add*` (per-format granularity), extraction, malformed-value suppression, filled-detection, the base-year relaxation, AND a `codegenReadsFragmentsIdentically` tri-check (interp == groovy-dynamic == java-static). Multiplatform (JVM + JS) eval parity: `interpreter.DateFragmentEvalTest`; loader: `interpreter.load.ModelLoaderTest.mapsDateFragmentTypeToDatePrecisionFragmentCarryingItsFormat`.
### IF58 — min/max over DATES ride the kernel's DateCombiner: no-value empty identity, valueless-operand skip, symmetric polarity, date-literal typing (FIXED 2026-07-07, graduated from IG38)

The WI-7 sweep's `IllegalStateException: incomparable values` crash class: `[DeliveryDate] < MinValue(Items* then PromiseDate)` over an all-empty date star made `Aggregates.min` fold to its **numeric** empty identity `Num(0)`, which then hit `ValCompare.language(Txt(date), Num(0))` — a type mixture no *legal* input can produce through comparison typing (the kernel types comparisons statically and rejects every date/text-vs-number shape; the mixture was interpreter-*made*). The mechanism: the interpreter's aggregates were **number-derived**, but the kernel dispatches min/max **statically by operand type onto two combiner families** — `NumberCombiner` (`minWert`/`min`) vs **`DateCombiner`** (`minWertDatum`/`minDatum`) — with different semantics (KERNEL-FINDINGS §6 "min/max over DATES"). Four sibling behaviors, all differential-locked:

- **Empty identity** — an all-empty date star (or zero rows) yields a NULL date; `vergleicheDATUM` returns `FALSE_OR_UNKNOWN` on a null side → the comparison does not fire. Interpreter: `Aggregates.min`/`max` take the fold identity as a parameter; the strategy passes `Val.Unknown` for a date-like aggregate ([`dateAggregate`] — any DATE/TIME/DATE_TIME operand decides, homogeneous by authoring) and `Num(0)` for numbers (IF51 unchanged). In a computation the no-value result is CLEARED, matching the kernel.
- **Operand-list emptiness** — `Min([OrderDate], [DeliveryDate])` with one date EMPTY: the kernel's `VkDate.combine` keeps the date-bearing side (empty operands are SKIPPED, unlike the number form's empty→0 substitution); a malformed/required-empty operand still poisons (nicht prüfungsrelevant). Interpreter: `minMaxOperandVals` drops empty (non-invalid) date-like field operands.
- **Symmetric polarity** — `DateCombiner` has no directional `kannGroesser/KleinerWerden`; it carries only the symmetric `nichtAngegeben` (any empty cell ∨ un-instantiated declared tail ∨ a `Having` filter), and a fired date comparison types `TRUE_AF` (OMISSION) on it regardless of direction. The number star's directional fill said VALUE for the cross directions (`> MinValue`, `< MaxValue`) — reproduced divergent, fixed by a date branch in `starFill` (`starOmission ∨ having != null`, symmetric).
- **Date-literal typing** — the kernel types a quoted literal DATETIME beside a date-valued `Min`/`MinValue`, so `Min([OD], [DD]) < "20.03.2026"` compares dates; `producesDate` now recognizes the min/max family over date operands, so `coerceDate` canonicalizes the literal.

**Locks:** `adapter.laws.DateAggregateDiffTest` — firing + polarity + compute (CLEARED vs the min date in the target's stored format) + a `codegenEvaluatesDateAggregatesIdentically` tri-check (both kernel strategies share the combiner runtime).

### IF59 — a semantic index is a PRESENCE operand too: `FieldFilled([X For k])`, quantifier operands, `FieldValueAsString` (FIXED 2026-07-07, graduated from IG38)

The sweep's `Expr$SemanticIndex` ClassCastException class: real rules use an indexed ref in presence positions — `FieldFilled(Count For "K1")`, `FieldNotFilled(…)`, `AllFieldsFilled([X For k], Y)`, and `FieldValueAsString(Count For "K1")` inside string concatenations — which the interpreter's `pathOf` hard-cast to `Expr.Field` (crash, sinking the whole model's validate/compute). Kernel semantics (source: `FieldFilled` compiles as a one-operand `AllFieldsFilled`; `ValidationCache.resolveSemanticIndex` → `IndexFieldCache.lookup` → `isWertAngegeben` on the ONE matched cell; locked by the differential):

- matched + check-relevant filled cell → **TRUE** (filled);
- an **ABSENT row**, an empty matched cell, or an empty/no-match KEY field → **FALSE** ("not filled" — an absent row is judged like an empty cell, NOT skipped, so `FieldNotFilled([X For "k"])` FIRES when no row carries the key);
- a malformed matched cell, or a formally invalid KEY field → **UNKNOWN** (not check-relevant; neither polarity fires).

Interpreter: `semanticIndexPresence` (the shared `presenceTri` now serves `FieldFilled`/`FieldNotFilled` AND `quantTally`, deduplicating the field 3-state); `FieldValueAsString` resolves the matched cell (absent/empty → `""`, which the `vergleicheSTRING` empty rule then suppresses; malformed → Unknown; a DATE-like indexed cell is unsupported-loud — its resolved `Val` is canonical ISO, not the raw stored form). **Locks:** `adapter.laws.SemanticIndexPresenceDiffTest` (presence × {literal, field-keyed} × {present, empty cell, absent row, malformed, empty key}, quantifier operands, `FieldValueAsString`, fired polarity).

### IF60 — the value-list quantifiers expand STARRED operands per cell; malformed handling is OP-ASYMMETRIC; index-field errors are field-local (FIXED 2026-07-07, graduated from IG38)

The sweep's biggest `Expr$Star` crash class: shape-B value-list quantifiers with a **starred FIELDS side** (`AtLeastOneFieldValueIncludedInValueList(Items* then Code In "A", "B")` — mm 26/26 crash instances, plus workspace compute preconditions) hit `pathOf`. The rewrite (`vlqSide`) expands BOTH sides per cell and reproduces the kernel's per-op classification EXACTLY (KERNEL-FINDINGS §8 "the value-list membership family" → the "Runtime semantics of the shape-B QUANTIFIERS" block); the old plain-field impl's blanket malformed-poison was itself wrong (believed unreachable — "shape-B lists are string-only" — but NUMBER field lists exist in the corpora). Kernel-exact rules now locked (`ValueListQuantifierStarDiffTest`):

- **`AtLeastOne`** skips empty/malformed cells and members outright (an empty value SET → no fire); **`No`** poisons on a malformed FIELDS cell or VALUES member; **`NotAll`** needs a filled fields cell, poisons only on the VALUES side.
- An **empty VALUES member** contributes nothing — never the number `0` (the old `evalExpr` empty-number substitution wrongly put `0` in the set) — and flags a fired `No`/`NotAll` OMISSION (`TRUE_AF`).
- Fired polarity: `No` is OMISSION on any empty fields cell / un-instantiated declared tail / empty values member; `NotAll` has NO fields-tail flag (the `No`-vs-`NotAll` asymmetry); `AtLeastOne` is VALUE.
- **Index-field errors are field-LOCAL:** a starred INDEX field's empty cell is mandatory-empty and EVERY duplicate-valued cell carries `uniqueIndex` — those cells are not check-relevant (UNKNOWN: skipped by `AtLeastOne`, poisoning `No`) — while a CLEAN SIBLING field in the same row reads normally (the row is not collectively compromised).
- A NUMBER field-list's value list is **field-valued only** (numeric literals are authoring-rejected), so the literal branch only ever carries strings.
- ~~A `Having`-filtered VLQ operand is unsupported-loud~~ — **since implemented and differential-locked (IF74, 2026-07-08)**: kernel-accepted on both sides, rows dropped before the per-cell classification, a Having-bearing operand forces OMISSION.

### IF61 — the per-rule degrade net: an unmodeled operand shape is UNSUPPORTED, never a crash that sinks the run (FIXED 2026-07-07, graduated from IG38)

IG38's meta-defect: `DmInterpreter.validateWith`'s per-rule tolerance catches only `UnsupportedConstructException`, so a `ClassCastException`/`IllegalStateException` escaping operand resolution lost the WHOLE model's coverage (the sweep tallied it as a model-level skip, distorting the divergence counts). The net is now: `pathOf` reports any non-`Expr.Field` operand in a field-reference position as unsupported (naming the construct), and a bare `Expr.Star` outside an aggregate likewise — so one exotic rule degrades to an `UnsupportedRule` entry while every other rule still evaluates. Deliberately NOT a blanket per-rule `catch(Throwable)`: a genuine invariant violation (e.g. an incomparable-value pair after IF58) stays loud — loud-not-wrong, and silent-wrong is the worst class. **Lock:** `interpreter.UnsupportedDegradeTest` (JVM+JS): a semantic index in a `FieldValuesNotUnique` operand degrades with the construct named; the healthy sibling rule still fires.

### IF62 — `RepetitionNotUnique` is a per-row 3VL predicate; a guard conjunct gates firing but NOT the duplicate cache (FIXED 2026-07-07, graduated from IG37)

The WI-7 sweep's largest *skip* class: `<guard> And RepetitionNotUnique(key)` — the RNU predicate as ONE conjunct of an And-tree, not the whole condition — was reported unsupported (44 distinct rules: 40 in kernel-mm, 4 in the workspaces; 92 rule-instances). The old recognizer (`DmInterpreter.repetitionNotUnique`) accepted only a *standalone* `RepetitionNotUnique(…)` as the entire condition — `if (ast !is Cond.Pred …) return null` — so any conjunctive guard fell through to the generic predicate path (`PredicateOp.byName("RepetitionNotUnique") == null` → `UnsupportedConstructException`). This was the whole IG37 class: the sweep found **zero** other null shapes — no RNU under `Or`, no nested RNU, no non-field key, and the other IG37 "candidates" (`@From`, composite/multi-key, a key under a non-repeatable sub-group) were already recognized correctly.

**The mechanism (clean-room, confirmed in BOTH strategies).** The kernel evaluates `RepetitionNotUnique` as a **per-row 3VL predicate** returning `TRUE_WF` (value duplicate → VALUE) / `TRUE_AF` (a key has a fillable-empty component → OMISSION) / `FALSE_OR_UNKNOWN`. Its duplicate cache is built **once over the full scope, INDEPENDENT of the guard**: runtime `RuntimeController.wiederholungNichtEindeutig` builds the cache on first call (`isInitialised`) by iterating ALL rows in `ermittleWiederholungenMitDuplikaten`, then per-row returns the 3VL result; codegen `EntityListCondition.st` emits a call to that SAME runtime method with a per-rule `RepetitionNotUniqueErrorCache`, so codegen delegates to the identical semantics (runtime-parity ⟹ codegen-parity, no separate combiner unlike IF58). The guard is therefore just a **separate conjunct combined by the ordinary 3VL `And`** — it gates whether *this* row fires, never which rows populate the cache, and its polarity combines (OMISSION if either operand is OMISSION).

**The fix (at the shared mechanism, not per-shape).** `repetitionNotUnique` now peels the top-level `And`-tree (`flattenAnd`): it recognizes the rule iff exactly ONE top-level conjunct is the RNU predicate, capturing the AND of the others as the `guard`. `repetitionNotUniqueMessages` keeps its full-scope duplicate detection (guard-independent, unchanged) and, for each duplicate row, evaluates the guard through a per-row `Interpreter` (`firedType`) — the row fires only if the guard fires, with the combined polarity; a FALSE/UNKNOWN guard (incl. a formally-invalid guard operand) suppresses it. Recognition stays **strict** — RNU under `Or`, nested below a non-`And`, more than one RNU conjunct, a non-field key, or a guard whose iteration scope is DEEPER than the keys' group all return null → the rule degrades loud (IF61), never silently wrong (none occurs in the corpora). The per-row `Interpreter` construction is now the shared `interpreterFor` helper used by both `rowOutcomes` and this pass.

**Sweep delta (both corpora):** the `RepetitionNotUnique` skip class went to **zero** — kernel-mm unsupported drops to `[186] custom-condition (no SPI)` only (was + `[83] RepetitionNotUnique`), workspaces loses its `[9]` RNU skip — while the divergence counts held EXACTLY (mm 47, workspaces 572): the 44 un-hidden rules match the kernel with no new divergence. (mm custom-condition rose 180→186 as ~6 rules whose *guard* is a `CustomCondition` now degrade under their real blocker — no SPI — rather than the RNU predicate: better classification, still loud.) **Locks:** `adapter.laws.RepetitionNotUniqueGuardedDiffTest` (guard gates firing but not the cache; omission-guard → OMISSION; comparison/multi-conjunct guards; a formally-invalid guard operand suppresses; an ancestor/shallower guard resolving per row; + a `codegenEvaluatesGuardedRnuIdentically` tri-check: interp == groovy-dynamic == static-JAVA) and multiplatform `interpreter.RepetitionNotUniqueGuardedTest` (JVM+JS — the same firing/polarity and the then-intentional refusal for RNU under `Or`). **Correction 2026-07-19:** the live kernel accepts RNU under `Or` and nested boolean placement, so that refusal is a legal-input gap, not a sound strict-recognition boundary; IG95 owns it.

### IF63 — `RuleGroup` is the rule's OWN group (`RegelKontext`); `GroupFilled(RuleGroup)` on an iteration group is per-row presence (FIXED 2026-07-07, graduated from IG40 (a)+(b))

The WI-7 sweep's **largest `validateFull` divergence class** — `GroupFilled(RuleGroup)` / `GroupNotFilled(RuleGroup)`, ~360 workspace instances.
