It **subsumed the row's reported "file/binary" sub-class (a)**: those were ordinary attachment rules `GroupFilled(RuleGroup) And NotExactlyOneFieldFilled(attachment_id, content)` over field NAMES (`content`/`attachment_id`) — **no file/binary field TYPE exists in the corpora** (the loader's type set is Number/String/Enum/Date/…; the sweep-session name was a field-name read), so (a) and (b) are ONE root. `RuleGroup` (the kernel's `RegelKontext`; KERNEL-FINDINGS "Condition directives — @SuppressWarning + RuleGroup") is a reserved group-context keyword for the rule's OWN group (the `ruleGroup` `CheckRegelKontextEntityImpl` resolves). The interpreter's parser had no case for it, so a **bare** `GroupFilled(RuleGroup)` — parsed via `parseAtom`'s identifier branch, which never reaches `readRef` — took `RuleGroup` as a child path `<base>/RuleGroup`, a nonexistent group, making `groupFilled` always FALSE. Effect: `GroupFilled(RuleGroup)` never fired (kernel-only misses) and `GroupNotFilled(RuleGroup)` always fired (interp-only extras — e.g. the mm `META_DATA_MISSING`), the two polarities of one bug.

**The mechanism (both strategies).** `RuleGroup` resolves at parse time to `base` (the rule group). The grammar admits it only as a standalone group reference — no descent segments, and a star draws `MVK_INVALID_WILDCARD_FOR_RULEGROUP` (the token exists solely for the nicer error). Its runtime meaning is the ordinary `GroupFilled` split-on-repeatability (KERNEL-FINDINGS §9 "GroupFilled splits on repeatability"): on a rule ITERATING a repeatable group, `GroupFilled(RuleGroup)` is the current row's presence — **TRUE for every instantiated row, even an all-empty one** (creating the row is content) — so it reads as an "is this repetition present" guard; on a NON-repeatable group it is the ordinary "has a filled descendant". `GroupNotFilled(RuleGroup)` on a repeatable iteration group is authoring-rejected (`MVK_NEG_CONDITION_IN_ITERATION`), so it occurs only on non-repeatable groups. Both kernel strategies share the parser's entity resolution ⟹ codegen-parity.

**The fix (at the parse root, not a per-shape patch).** The parser resolves the reserved `RuleGroup` keyword to `Expr.Field(base)` in BOTH parse paths — `parseAtom`'s bare-identifier branch (the common `GroupFilled(RuleGroup)`) and `readRef` (the `[RuleGroup]` / `$RuleGroup` forms). `matchKeyword`/equality enforces a word boundary, so a field named `RuleGroupX` is unaffected. No eval change was needed — `groupFilled(base)` already had the exact per-row / non-repeatable semantics; the defect was purely the mis-parse, so the whole class (both predicates, repeatable + non-repeatable, standalone + conjunctive) is fixed at once. **Locks:** `adapter.laws.RuleGroupSelfReferenceDiffTest` (repeatable per-row presence incl. the empty-row fire, the attachment shape, a value-compare conjunct, non-repeatable filled/empty for both predicates, + a static-JAVA tri-check on the attachment shape) and multiplatform `interpreter.RuleGroupSelfReferenceTest` (JVM+JS). Sweep delta filled in with IF64 below.

### IF64 — `RepetitionNotUnique` excludes a row whose KEY cell is FORMALLY INVALID (per-key-cell, not row-wide) (FIXED 2026-07-07, graduated from IG40 (c))

IG40(c) — the "`RepetitionNotUnique` × `uniqueIndex`" interaction. The interpreter's RNU built each row's duplicate key from the raw `displayValue` of the key cells with **no formal-validity gate**, so a formally-invalid key cell — a malformed value, or a duplicate-INDEX (`uniqueIndex`) cell — was still counted as a duplicate *value* and the interpreter fired RNU where the kernel did not (interp-only extras). The clearest instance: `RepetitionNotUnique(<indexField>)` over a duplicated index — the auto `uniqueIndex` check makes each index cell "unknown" (IF15) yet the interpreter also fired the RNU on those same cells.

**The mechanism (both strategies).** The kernel builds its duplicate cache from **check-relevant cells only**: runtime `RuntimeController.getWerteOrNullIfEinFeldNichtPruefungsRelevant` returns null — the row is `continue`-skipped in `ermittleWiederholungenMitDuplikaten` — the moment ANY key cell's `isWertAngegeben` is **UNKNOWN** (= `!isPruefungsRelevant` = the cell is marked `fehlerhaft`). The check consults only the key `entityList`, so the exclusion is **per-KEY-CELL, not row-wide** — a formal error on a NON-key field of the same row does not drop it (differentially confirmed: `RNU(Count)` still fires on rows whose INDEX `Sku` is a duplicate). Codegen `EntityListCondition.st` calls the same runtime method ⟹ codegen-parity (IF62). This is the exact sibling of the field-local `uniqueIndex` suppression of ordinary referencing rules (IF15).

**Which key cells are UNKNOWN — CORRECTED 2026-07-07 (the warm-up differential; supersedes the original carve-out).** The original IF64 claimed a required-**EMPTY** cell is **FALSE** (a valid empty participant → a null key component), NOT UNKNOWN — so `repetitionNotUniqueMessages` excluded `MANDATORY_FIELD` from the row-drop. That was **source-grounded but not differentially verified, and it was wrong.** The composite-key differential — key `(Sku, Count)`, `Sku` required + empty on both rows, `Count` duplicated — proved the kernel fires **nothing** while the interpreter fired OMISSION on both. Mechanism: the kernel's mandatory check is a **PRELIMINARY autogen rule** (`CodeGenRuleDefinition.isPreliminary()` — *"exactly the auto generated rules are preliminary"* — → `controller.preliminaryError(...)` → `markFieldAsInvalidForValidation`) that runs BEFORE the RNU rule builds its cache, so a required-empty key cell is already `fehlerhaft` ⟹ `isWertAngegeben == UNKNOWN` ⟹ the row is dropped. So **every** cause `formalErrorAt` reports is UNKNOWN: a bad FORMAT, a custom-type rejection, a duplicate-INDEX (`uniqueIndex`) cell, an over-repetition cell, **and a required-EMPTY cell**. The one key cell that is still a **valid empty participant** (`isWertAngegeben == FALSE` → null component → the OMISSION-on-duplicate case) is an **OPTIONAL-empty** cell — a non-required field left empty, for which `formalErrorAt` is null. The distinguishing property is **requiredness, not emptiness.** (Kernel fact: KERNEL-FINDINGS §9 supplement, corrected same day.)

**The fix (at the shared root, not the uniqueIndex symptom).** `repetitionNotUniqueMessages` drops a row from the duplicate cache when any key cell is formally invalid — reusing the single "is this operand unknown?" source `formalErrorAt` (format / custom-type / `UNIQUE_INDEX` / over-repetition / `MANDATORY_FIELD`), i.e. `formalErrorAt != null` with **no carve-out** (the 2026-07-07 correction removed the `!= MANDATORY_FIELD` exception). A malformed key cell is excluded the same way — a second UNKNOWN cause proving the root, not a dup-index special-case. IF15's field-local suppression of ordinary referencing rules is unchanged (probe-confirmed still correct here). **Locks:** `adapter.laws.RepetitionNotUniqueFormalKeyDiffTest` (RNU over a duplicated index → no fire; a malformed key → no fire; a **composite key with a required-empty component → no fire** + a fully-duplicated control → VALUE; the clean-non-key `RNU(Count)` control still fires; mixed rows; + a static-JAVA codegen tri-check), the OPTIONAL-empty-participant control `adapter.laws.RepetitionNotUniqueDiffTest.compositeEmptyComponentIsOmission`, and multiplatform `interpreter.RepetitionNotUniqueFormalKeyTest` (JVM+JS, incl. the required-empty case).

**Sweep delta (both corpora, IG40 fully closed — IF63 + IF64):** the `GroupFilled`/`GroupNotFilled(RuleGroup)` divergence class is **gone** — **workspaces 572→484 (−88), kernel-mm 47→32 (−15)**, with **no divergence count rising** (the drop is the fixed class; the un-hidden RuleGroup rules now match the kernel). The drop is entirely IF63 (the RuleGroup class was ~360 workspace rule-instances); IF64's RNU-formal-key divergence barely seeds in the corpus (a duplicate index is rare under the seeder), so it is locked by the own-domain DiffTest, not a sweep instance. A residual scan confirms **zero** `GroupFilled/GroupNotFilled(RuleGroup)` divergences remain: the blocks still flagged for containing "RuleGroup" diverge on a DIFFERENT conjunct — either `NoGroupFilled(<starred group>)` (an interp-only over-fire, a separate class) or `CurrentRepetition(RuleGroup)` inside a `$`-outer-correlation `Having` (the date-range-overlap idiom, a distinct `$`-correlation construct) — both recorded as a new gap bounced to WI-T, neither introduced by this fix. The remaining 484/32 are the other WI-T rows (IG39 computations, IG41 mm-structural).

### IF65 — a GUARDED / TIERED computation is a validation rule too: the implicit validation is generated for every named computation, at the right per-row pointer (FIXED 2026-07-07, graduated from IG39)

The WI-7 sweep's computation class: on real subtype models, the kernel fires a computation's implicit validation (IF32 — `validateFull` flags a stored computed value that disagrees with what the computation would produce) even when the computation is **guarded** (a `commonPrecondition` / a per-alternative `precondition`, e.g. `AtLeastOneFieldFilled`/`AllFieldsFilled`) or **tiered** (>1 alternative). The interpreter's IF32 synthesized the implicit validation ONLY for the degenerate **single-unconditional** computation (`FieldFilled(F) And [F] != op`) and returned null for every guarded/tiered one — a kernel-only miss across most real computations.

**The mechanism (source, both strategies).** The kernel builds ONE validation rule per computation in the md-model (`ValidationModelConverter.getComputationStatement` → `ComputationAlternativesJoiner.join()`): a **field gate** `FieldFilled(F) And`, then the optional `(commonPrecondition) And`, then the alternatives — a single alternative is `[(precondition) And] [F] != <operation>`; **>1** alternatives are the `Or` of each so-gated comparison `( (pre0 And [F] != op0) Or (pre1 And [F] != op1) … )`. So the implicit validation is a **normal rule** gated by the SAME precondition selection the compute path uses — the "two faces" of IG39 are one mechanism: the guard that clears the field on `compute` also gates the validation. The compute clear/precondition decision itself was **already at parity** (IF21 / `ComputationPreconditionDiffTest`; probe-confirmed guard-TRUE→value, guard-FALSE→cleared on both engines), so IG39's compute face was NOT a divergence — only the validation FIRING was missing. Both kernel strategies share the md-model-generated rule ⟹ codegen-parity (KERNEL-FINDINGS §11/§12 supplement).

**The fix (AST-direct, at the shared root).** `implicitComputationCond` builds the implicit-validation `Cond` AST **directly from the computation's already-parsed alternatives** (`parsedOf`) — the field gate + common + the single/`Or`-of-alternatives comparison — rather than synthesizing a condition STRING and re-parsing it (the first attempt did the latter and introduced parser crashes on complex operations, an IF61 whole-model sink; building the AST from the parsed components reuses the exact operand ASTs compute uses and cannot fail to re-parse). It rides the existing rule machinery (`condAstOf` consults the precomputed `implicitComputationAst` map), so the `!=` polarity, empty-as-0, iteration, and suppression are the engine's own. **Two sub-fixes:** (1) the synthetic rule's `errorEntityRelPath` is `../<fRel>` (written FROM the rule element `<group>/<name>`, a sibling of the computed field), correcting a **latent IF32 pointer bug** — the old bare `fRel` was read relative to the rule ELEMENT by `resolveErrorPath`, appending a spurious `<name>` segment to a **repeatable-group** computation's per-row pointer (never caught: no lock checked a repeatable computation's implicit-validation pointer, only the code); (2) the recognizer accepts a computation with any number of alternatives (only unnamed / zero-alternative computations are skipped). *Locked:* `adapter.laws.ComputationGuardedValidationDiffTest` — commonPrecondition / precondition-on-alt / multi-alternative-tiered / a per-row REPEATABLE-group computation, firing + message type + per-row pointer + a static-JAVA codegen tri-check; and multiplatform `interpreter.GuardedComputationValidationTest` (JVM+JS — guarded + tiered firing).

**Sweep delta (workspaces 484→336, −148; kernel-mm 32 unchanged):** the guarded/tiered computation-validations now fire and match the kernel (firing + per-row pointer), collapsing the `validateFull both-sides-differ` class from 269→104. kernel-mm holds at 32 (its 6 computations sit in multi-root `header`/`content` models — IG41). **Two residual classes surfaced and were recorded + bounced to WI-T (§5 divergence protocol — not rushed in-session):** (a) **IG43** — a GENERAL comparison-polarity gap: `[F] != <arithmetic RHS with an empty operand>` types **VALUE** in the interpreter where the kernel types **OMISSION** (root: `Interpreter.fillOf` doesn't propagate the kernel's `VkBigDecimal` fill through `Expr.Arith`; MUL/DIV/POW are sign-dependent). It is NOT computation-specific — a plain rule `[Back] != [Qty] + [Stock(empty)]` diverges identically — but it surfaces widely via the computation-validations (`[F] != <operation>`), and is the bulk of the residual `validateFull both-sides-differ`. (b) the residual **compute** value divergences (unchanged by this fix, so not the guarded-clear mechanism — probe-confirmed at parity) — a distinct compute-value class awaiting its own characterization.

### IF66 — a computation's implicit-validation pointer follows its computed field even ACROSS subtrees / roots (FIXED 2026-07-07, graduated from IG41 — multi-root error-entity path rendering)

The WI-7 sweep's **multi-root pointer class** (IG41's generalization target): on a `header`/`content` multi-root model a computation can compute a field in **another top-level root** (e.g. the mm `CALC_MODEL_TYPE`/`CALC_MODEL_VERSION` computations, sitting under `/content`, write `/header/modelType`/`/header/modelVersion` via a `computedFieldRelPath` of `../../header/…`). The implicit validation (IF65) then FIRED correctly but pointed at a **mangled, over-long** path: the kernel renders `/header[1]/modelType` (the actual field), the interpreter rendered `/content[1]//header[1]/modelType` — a `both-sides-differ` (same code+type, wrong pointer).

**The mechanism (clean-room, the locus + the invariant it broke).** `implicitComputationRuleDef` built the synthetic error-entity relpath as `"../" + comp.targetPath.removePrefix(comp.group + "/")` — which **assumed the computed field is a descendant of the computation's group**. When the target is NOT under the group, `removePrefix` no-ops and leaves the **absolute** `targetPath`, so the relpath became `"../" + "/header/modelType"` = `..//header/modelType` (a double slash). `resolveErrorPath` popped the one `../` to `/content` then appended `/header/modelType`, yielding `/content//header/modelType`; `formatPointer` split the empty segment into a phantom indexed level → the 4-part mangle. The broken invariant: *the implicit validation's error entity is the computed field's absolute location, independent of the computation's iteration group* — the kernel resolves the DM-JSON `computedFieldRelPath` from the computation NODE (so `../../header/modelType` reaches the sibling root), and the interpreter must resolve to the identical absolute path. (The kernel fact is homed: KERNEL-FINDINGS §11/§12 supplement — "Where the implicit rule FIRES".)

**Second instance (predicted + reproduced BEFORE the fix — confirms the mechanism, not the symptom).** The true root is broader than "multi-root": it is **"the computed field is not a descendant of the computation's group."** A **single-root** model with sibling subgroups reproduces it identically — a computation under `/Root/A` writing `/Root/B/field` (`../../B/field`) mangled to `/Root[1]/A[1]//Root[1]/B[1]/field` where the kernel renders `/Root[1]/B[1]/field`. So multi-root is one common source (target in another root); the sibling-subtree is the other.

**The fix (at the shared root — a general relative path).** `implicitComputationRuleDef` now sets the error-entity relpath to `relativePath("${comp.group}/$name", comp.targetPath)` — the general `../…`-form path from the rule element to the **absolute** target, matching `resolveErrorPath`'s `../`-pop convention (one `../` per rule-element segment above the two paths' common prefix, then the target's tail). This resolves to the exact target for **all three** shapes — a descendant (unchanged: `/Order/FEE` → `/Order/EffectiveFee` stays `../EffectiveFee`), a sibling subtree, and a cross-root target — so the descendant/repeatable cases the earlier form handled are preserved bit-for-bit while the cross-subtree mangle is gone. **All pointer consumers checked:** `compute` was never mangled (it renders `comp.targetPath` directly, verified equal to the kernel here); `validateFull` and `validatePart` both route through `resolveErrorPath`, so the one relpath fix covers them. Both kernel strategies build the implicit validation from the same md-model computation rule ⟹ codegen-parity (static-JAVA tri-checked). **Locks:** `adapter.laws.MultiRootComputationPointerDiffTest` (cross-root + sibling-subtree validateFull pointer/type, cross-root compute pointer/value, + a static-JAVA codegen tri-check) and multiplatform `interpreter.MultiRootComputationPointerTest` (JVM+JS — both pointer shapes + the cross-root compute pointer).

**Sweep delta (kernel-mm 32→22, −10; workspaces 336→315, −21).** kernel-mm: the `CALC_MODEL_TYPE`/`CALC_MODEL_VERSION` `both-sides-differ` pair on the two mapping metamodels (5 seeds each) is gone. workspaces: −21 `validateFull both-sides-differ` (104→83) — the cross-root computation-validations on the `_CDM` composite (multi-root) models. **IG44 interplay characterized:** the workspaces `compute both-sides-differ` (154) is **unchanged** by this validateFull-only fix, so IG44's "multi-root pointer candidate" is resolved for the *validation* pointer but its **compute-VALUE** residual is a genuinely distinct class (not the pointer mangle) — IG44 stays open, narrowed. **Residual after this fix** is the other IG41 classes (`stelligkeitZuLang`, selection-model default-consistency), IG43 (arithmetic-empty polarity), IG44 (compute-value), and out-of-scope classes the mm exercises (custom-ref error-entity attribution IG8; the metamodels' own authored `MVK_*`/`COMPUTATION_MISSING`/`NO_VAL_AND_HINT_LISTS` rules).

### IF67 — `stelligkeitZuLang` is a GENERIC > 15-total-digit parse check on every number; the amount integer-cap is `zahlZuvieleVorkommastellen` (FIXED 2026-07-07, graduated from IG41 — stelligkeitZuLang class)

The WI-7 sweep's `stelligkeitZuLang` K-only miss (IG41 class 2): the kernel fired `stelligkeitZuLang` on number fields the interpreter left clean. The interpreter had **misattributed** the code — it fired `stelligkeitZuLang` only for a `trait:"amount"` field whose INTEGER-digit count exceeded `15 − scale`, and had **no generic digit check at all**. Two kernel facts, confirmed clean-room from source (KERNEL-FINDINGS §3 supplement):

- **`stelligkeitZuLang` = > 15 TOTAL digits** (integer + fractional, sign and decimal separator excluded), a GENERIC parse-time sanity check on **every** number (`NumberConversion.parseNumber` → `sanityCheck`; `FormatDefinitionZahl.validiereFormat`), NOT trait-gated. It fires **third** — right after the plus-sign and invalid-character checks and **before** the sign / scale / leading-zero / integer-cap checks (probe-verified it beats signed-zero, leading-zeros, and the decimal-separator check).
- **The `trait:"amount"` integer-cap fires `zahlZuvieleVorkommastellen`, NOT `stelligkeitZuLang`** — the kernel's `TypeConverter` maps BOTH an explicit `maxIntegerDigits` AND the amount `15 − maxFractionalDigits` to the one `maxDigitsBeforeDecimalPlace` cap, which emits `zahlZuvieleVorkommastellen`. Amount is not a distinct code; the interpreter's separate `AMOUNT_PRECISION_TOO_LONG` code was a phantom.

**Decisive differential (the second instance that broke the old model).** amount `scale=5`, value `12345678901` (11 integer, 0 fractional, 11 total): total 11 ≤ 15 so `stelligkeitZuLang` does **not** fire, but 11 > `15−5=10` so the kernel fires **`zahlZuvieleVorkommastellen`** — where the interpreter fired `stelligkeitZuLang`. This is the case where the two checks disagree, and it proved the amount cap's code was wrong (not merely that a check was missing).

**The fix.** (1) Added the generic total-digit check to `numberFormalError` (`raw.count { it.isDigit } > 15 → stelligkeitZuLang`), positioned right after the invalid-number check so its parse-time precedence matches the kernel. (2) Folded the amount integer-cap into `TOO_MANY_INTEGER_DIGITS` (`zahlZuvieleVorkommastellen`): `numberConstraintError` now uses `maxIntegerDigits ?: amountMaxIntegerDigits` as one cap firing the one code, and the phantom `AMOUNT_PRECISION_TOO_LONG` enum was repurposed to `TOTAL_DIGITS_TOO_LONG` (`stelligkeitZuLang`). Because `numberFormalError` runs before `numberConstraintError`, an amount value with > 15 total digits correctly hits the generic check first (matching the kernel). **Locks:** `adapter.laws.FieldConstraintFormalDiffTest` (plain/scale-2 > 15 total; amount integer-cap `zahlZuvieleVorkommastellen` at total ≤ 15; the total-digit check beating leading-zeros and the decimal-separator check) and multiplatform `interpreter.NumberDigitFormalTest` (JVM+JS — both codes + precedence). **Sweep delta: kernel-mm 22→19** (the 3 `stelligkeitZuLang` K-only misses gone), workspaces unchanged (its seeded values stay within 15 digits).

### IF68 — a group-count quantifier over a STARRED group counts the group's INSTANTIATED rows (FIXED 2026-07-07, graduated from IG41 selection-model class + IG42's group-count over-fire)

The WI-7 sweep's selection-model default-consistency class (IG41) and the workspaces `NoGroupFilled(<starred group>)` over-fire (IG42's first bullet) were **one root**. A group-count quantifier over a **starred repeatable group** — `NoGroupFilled(G*)`, `AtLeastOneGroupFilled(G*)` (the kernel's `Selected*`/`Unselected*` selection idiom: `[Default] == "Unselected" And NoGroupFilled(Selected*) And AtLeastOneGroupFilled(Unselected*)`) — was mis-evaluated.

**The mechanism (clean-room, locus + invariant).** `Interpreter.quantCounts` routed a single `Expr.Star` operand to `starQuantTally` — the FIELD-list tally, which takes `group = star.path.substringBeforeLast('/')` (the group's PARENT) and counts a per-row *field* cell. For a starred GROUP the star's path IS the group (`/Sel/Selected`), so it resolved the parent `/Sel` and counted **zero** group rows. Broken invariant: *a starred group operand's tally is over the GROUP's rows, not a field within its parent.* The zero count made `NoGroupFilled(G*)` (fires at `f == n`) **over-fire** (0==0 true even when the group has rows) and `AtLeastOneGroupFilled(G*)` (fires at `t >= 1`) **under-fire** — the two polarity-opposite symptoms of the one bug (the over-fire is what IG42 saw in the workspaces; the under-fire is what made the mm selection rules a K-only miss, since the whole `And` collapsed).

**The "filled group row" semantics (probe-verified, both strategies).** Every INSTANTIATED row counts as a "filled" group — a created-but-empty row is content (IF46, matching `NumberOfFilledGroups(G*)`): the kernel does **not** fire `NoGroupFilled(G*)` on one instantiated-but-empty row, and **does** fire `AtLeastOneGroupFilled(G*)`. So the tally is `t = n = instantiated (Having-matching) rows`, `f = 0`. The kernel forbids `*` on a group for the `AllGroupsFilled`/`NotAllGroupsFilled`/collectively family (`MVK_NO_WILDCARDS_G_ALLOWED`), so only the count-zero / count-≥1 predicates reach this path on a valid model. **Polarity** also needed a starred-group case: the error is fixed by ADDING a row (`(t,f,n) → (t+1,f,n+1)`), not by filling a fixed-arity operand (`(1..f)`, which is empty when `f=0`) — so `NoGroupFilled(G*)` at zero rows is **OMISSION** (add a row) and `AtLeastOneGroupFilled(G*)` is **VALUE** (adding never clears it), matching the kernel.

**The fix (at the shared root).** A new `Interpreter.starGroupTally` tallies the group's rows (`ctxOf(star).rowContexts(star.path)`, filtered by a `Having`), returning `(n, 0, n)`; `quantCounts` routes a countsGroups predicate's single star to it (the field-list `starQuantTally` stays for field quantifiers). `predType` gets the add-a-row fillability for a starred-group operand. Both kernel strategies share the runtime group tally ⟹ codegen-parity (static-JAVA tri-checked). **Locks:** `adapter.laws.StarredGroupQuantifierDiffTest` (both predicates × zero / filled / instantiated-empty rows, the combined selection-DEFAULT shape, + a static-JAVA codegen tri-check) and multiplatform `interpreter.StarredGroupQuantifierTest` (JVM+JS — the tally + polarity). **Sweep delta: kernel-mm 19→0 — COMPLETE PARITY** (the group-count bug had cascaded through most of the group-heavy metamodel rules, so closing it collapsed the whole residual); workspaces `validateFull both-sides-differ` 83→57 (−26 — the `NoGroupFilled` over-fire component removed from those instances, which stay divergent only for their IG43/IG44 rules; total held at 315). *(The "requiredness firing on a required meta-group" hypothesis IG41 also listed did NOT surface as a distinct divergence — kernel-mm reaching complete parity via this one fix confirms there is no residual requiredness class.)*

### IF69 — `VkBigDecimal` fill propagates through ARITHMETIC: an empty operand can make a fired comparison OMISSION (FIXED 2026-07-07, graduated from IG43)

The WI-7 sweep's arithmetic-empty polarity class: a fired comparison over an arithmetic RHS with an EMPTY operand — `[F] != [A] + [B]` with `B` empty — types **VALUE** in the interpreter where the kernel types **OMISSION** (the RHS can still grow by filling `B`). **General, NOT computation-specific** — a plain authored rule diverges identically — but it surfaces via the per-computation implicit validation (`[F] != <operation>`, IF65/IF32), where an arithmetic operation is the norm.

**The mechanism (clean-room, both strategies).** The kernel's `VkBigDecimal` carries the directional fill bits `kannGroesserWerden`/`kannKleinerWerden` on the value, and every arithmetic operation PROPAGATES them (`add`/`subtract`/`multiply`/`divide`/`power`), so `[A]+[B]` with `B` an empty (fillable) field keeps the RHS fillable → `BedingungsOperatorHelper.vergleiche` types the fired comparison `TRUE_AF` (OMISSION). `Interpreter.fill` had **no `Expr.Arith` case** — it fell to the symmetric-leaf `else` (`leafOmission(Arith)` = false), so an arithmetic operand read as a **fixed** value → always VALUE. Both kernel strategies execute the SAME runtime `VkBigDecimal` arithmetic (the Groovy/Java codegen emits calls to it, does not inline the arithmetic) ⟹ codegen-parity (static-JAVA tri-checked).

**The five ops' propagation (truth table, derived clean-room from `VkBigDecimal`; `g`/`s` = canGrow/canShrink):**

| op | canGrow | canShrink | note |
|---|---|---|---|
| `+` | `a.g ∨ b.g` | `a.s ∨ b.s` | straight combine |
| `−` | `a.g ∨ b.s` | `a.s ∨ b.g` | the **subtrahend's direction flips** |
| `×` | `produktKannGroesser` | `produktKannKleiner` | **SIGN-DEPENDENT**: `(a.g∧b.g) ∨ (a.s∧b.s) ∨ (b>0∧a.g) ∨ (b<0∧a.s) ∨ (a>0∧b.g) ∨ (a<0∧b.s)` (grow); mirror for shrink — needs each operand's resolved value SIGN |
| `÷` | `a × (1/b)` | — | the reciprocal `1/b` has b's sign + flipped fillability (`g = b.s ∨ (b<0∧b.g)`, `s = b.g ∨ (b>0∧b.s)`) |
| `^` | `powCanChange` | — | fixed-exponent (a square of a signed-empty base grows, can't shrink below 0), variable-exponent-fixed-base (monotone by base vs 0/1/−1 and exponent parity), everything-variable, negative-exponent reciprocal recursion |

**The fix (at the shared root, IF10 runtime layer).** A pure `ArithFill` object (sibling of `Polarity`/`Aggregates`) holds the propagation; `Interpreter.fill` gains `is Expr.Arith -> arithFill(e)`, which resolves each operand's `Fill` (recursive — nesting flows through) plus its value sign (`×`/`÷`) or value (`^`), and dispatches. A `+` over STRING operands is concatenation (no `VkBigDecimal` fill) → the fixed leaf; a malformed / non-numeric operand (div-by-zero, Unknown) → the fixed leaf (the term is Unknown, the comparison does not fire, its type is discarded — matching `INVALID_NUMBER`). **Sibling class (fixed next, IF71):** arithmetic nested inside a *value function* — `RoundAccounting([a]+[b], n)`, `AbsValue(...)` — routed through `funcFill`'s generic `else → leafOmission`, which does not recurse into `Expr.Arith`, so a function-wrapped fillable arith was not propagated (the kernel's `VkBigDecimal.setScale`/`abs` DO carry the flags — probe-confirmed `[F] != RoundAccounting([A]+[B], 0)` diverges kernel OMISSION / interp VALUE). This is the dominant corpus shape (money computations round), which is why this fix's own workspaces instance delta is small; IF71 closed the function-wrapped propagation.

*Locked:* `adapter.laws.ArithmeticFillPolarityDiffTest` (the five ops × empty/signed/unsigned/nested operands, the sign-dependent `×` flip both ways, a plain authored rule AND the per-computation implicit validation, + a static-JAVA codegen tri-check) and multiplatform `interpreter.ArithmeticFillTest` (JVM+JS — all five ops). Kernel fact: KERNEL-FINDINGS §1/§3 supplement "fill propagates through arithmetic".

**Sweep delta (true before→after, workspaces + kernel-mm both re-run).** workspaces `validateFull both-sides-differ` **57→56** (−1), total **315→314**; every other class UNCHANGED (`kernel-only` 50, `interp-only` 6, all `compute` classes 153/39/10) — **no regression** (a type-only fix cannot alter firing, confirmed). kernel-mm stays **0**. The delta is small because (a) the sweep counts diverging INSTANCES, and most also diverge for a co-occurring reason, and (b) the corpus's arithmetic computation-validations are overwhelmingly **function-wrapped** (RoundAccounting), the IG45 sibling this fix does not reach. IG43's value is the CORRECTNESS of the arithmetic-RHS polarity (kernel-differential-proven, JVM+JS + codegen), not the corpus footprint.

### IF70 — the NUMBER formal-error precedence follows the kernel's `FormatDefinitionZahl` sequence exactly (FIXED 2026-07-07, graduated from IG42's number-formal-precedence class)

The WI-7 sweep's number formal-precedence class: on the same seeded value the kernel emitted `zahlOhneDezimalTrenner` where the interpreter emitted `zahlIstNull`. Root-caused (both strategies) to TWO mis-orderings against the kernel's fixed `FormatDefinitionZahl.validiereFormat` ladder (KERNEL-FINDINGS §3 "the full NUMBER formal-error precedence order"): the interpreter's `numberConstraintError` checked **`zeroNotAllowed` FIRST** (kernel step 10, but it ran before the missing-separator/wrong-decimals/integer-cap checks, steps 7-9), and **`LEADING_ZEROS` fired in the parse-level `numberFormalError`** (so before every declared-constraint check, where the kernel fires it LAST, step 11). The formal checks are a pre-rule conversion step (`formalePruefung` — the runtime `TypeConverter`, not the generated rule code), so both kernel strategies share the ladder ⟹ codegen-parity.

**Sibling sweep — precedence bugs come in families (the drill).** The reported `zahlIstNull`-vs-`zahlOhneDezimalTrenner` pair was one symptom; the misplaced `LEADING_ZEROS` produced FOUR more divergences of the same class (leading-zeros wrongly beating the decimal-separator, wrong-decimals, integer-cap, and zero-not-allowed checks), plus a second zero-not-allowed pair (`0.0` → the kernel's `zahlFalscheAnzahlNachkommastellen`). All six were reproduced against the real kernel and locked as a precedence MATRIX.

**The fix.** Moved `LEADING_ZEROS` out of `numberFormalError` into `numberConstraintError` at its correct LATE position (after `zeroNotAllowed`), and moved `ZERO_NOT_ALLOWED` after the min-fractional-digits and integer-cap checks — so `numberConstraintError` now runs the kernel's exact order: missing-separator → too-few-decimals → integer-cap → zero-not-allowed → leading-zeros → length → range. `numberFormalError` keeps the parse-level ladder (plus / invalid / > 15 total / signed-zero / scale-0-separator / too-many-decimals). Single-check behavior is preserved (each still fires alone — control-tested). **Locks:** `adapter.laws.FieldConstraintFormalDiffTest` (the 6-case precedence matrix, each pinning the kernel winner AND the differential) + multiplatform `interpreter.NumberDigitFormalTest` (the matrix + single-check controls, JVM+JS).

**Sweep delta (true before→after, both corpora re-run).** workspaces `validateFull both-sides-differ` **56→53** (−3): on those 3 instances the interpreter's WRONG formal code is now corrected to the kernel's, so the interp-only component of their signature-diff disappears and they reclassify to `kernel-only` (50→53, +3) — they retain a co-occurring kernel-only miss (IF71 / the `$`-correlation). **No regression** — `interp-only` stays 6, every `compute` class 153/39/10, and kernel-mm 0, total held at 314 (a which-code-wins reorder cannot make the interpreter fire a code the kernel does not). IG44 (compute-value) is untouched, as expected for a validateFull-only fix.

### IF71 — `VkBigDecimal` fill propagates through the VALUE FUNCTIONS too: Round preserves, Abs transforms, Min/Max combine, RangeAsNumber is grow-only (FIXED 2026-07-07, graduated from IG45)

IF69's sibling, at the same `Fill`-propagation root but one level up: a fired comparison over a **value function wrapping a fillable operand** — `[F] != RoundAccounting([A]+[B], n)` with `B` empty — typed **VALUE** in the interpreter where the kernel types **OMISSION**. This is the **dominant** corpus shape (money computations round, so the per-computation implicit validation `[F] != <operation>` is almost always function-wrapped — IF65/IF69). `Interpreter.funcFill` routed every value function through the generic `else → leafOmission`, which (a) does not recurse into an `Expr.Arith` arg (so a `Round*(<arith>, n)` lost the arithmetic fill → always VALUE) and (b) is symmetric (so a `Round*Value`/`AbsValue`/`RangeAsNumber` over an UNSIGNED empty field mis-typed the `≥`/`≤` direction OMISSION).

**The mechanism (clean-room, both strategies), per value function — each derived from its `VkBigDecimal` op:**

| function | kernel op | fill rule |
|---|---|---|
| `RoundDown/Up/Accounting([expr], n)` + `*Value(field)` | `setScale` | **PRESERVES** `kannGroesser`/`kannKleiner` exactly → `fill(arg0)` unchanged (the places literal is fixed) |
| `Abs([expr])` / `AbsValue(field)` | `abs` | **TRANSFORMS** with the operand's value sign: `g' = g∨s`, `s' = (pos∧s)∨(neg∧g)` — `\|x\|` grows off either direction, shrinks only toward 0 (so `\|x\| ≥ 0` never shrinks → `> −n` is VALUE) |
| `Min(…)` / `Max(…)` (operand-list) | `min`/`max` | **COMBINE** directionally, folded over the operands: min shrinks via EITHER operand and grows via the SMALLER (equal → both); max mirrors — needs the operands' value ORDER |
| `RangeAsNumber(field, s, e)` | `bereichAlsZahl` | **GROW-ONLY** (`kannKleiner=false`, a value ≥ 0; `kannGroesser` = the source string is empty) — the count-aggregate shape |

`VkBigDecimal.setScale`/`abs`/`min`/`max`/`bereichAlsZahl` are the SAME runtime both strategies emit calls to (the Groovy/Java codegen does not inline them) ⟹ codegen-parity (static-JAVA tri-checked).

**The fix (at the shared IF10 runtime layer).** `ArithFill` (renamed in spirit to the `VkBigDecimal` numeric-op fill: the five binary ops + `abs`/`min`/`max`) gains `abs(fill, valueSign)`, `min(a, b, less)`, `max(a, b, less)`; `Interpreter.funcFill` adds the four cases — Round → `fill(f.args[0])`, Abs → `ArithFill.abs(fill(arg0), signOf(arg0))`, Min/Max → a value-ordered left-fold `minMaxFill`, RangeAsNumber → `Fill.of(canGrow = leafOmission(arg0), canShrink = false)`. The strategy still only **resolves + dispatches**; the per-op propagation is pure runtime. `SumOfProducts` (fill-carrier via `multiply`/`add`) was verified against the differential to MATCH the current symmetric leaf for its reachable (signed rep-list) operands — a directional divergence needs an unsigned rep-list field (none in the fixture), so it is not touched (its symmetric leaf is correct for every reachable case). `FieldValueAsNumber` over a free string is rejected at codegen (`MVK_NO_CONVERT_IN_NUMBER`) — parity-by-construction; for its valid (number-convertible / numeric-enum) forms `feldWertAlsZahl`'s fill is symmetric = the leaf.

**Sweep also surfaced a DISTINCT root — the empty-operand FIRING gap (fixed separately in IF73).** `Length` over an empty string and the date/time extractors + date diffs over empty operands resolved to `Val.Unknown` in the interpreter (`evalFunc`'s `dateComponent`/`timeComponent`/`epochOf`/`Length` → Unknown) and were SUPPRESSED, whereas the kernel's `laenge`/`getDatumsTeil` return a fillable **0** (`nichtAngegeben` preserved) and FIRE — a false-negative, not a fill-polarity mistype. That is `evalFunc` value-resolution, a different locus than `funcFill`, so it was fixed as its own finding (IF73): the empty-operand-0 coercion + `Length` grow-only / extractors-and-diffs symmetric.

*Locked:* `adapter.laws.FunctionFillPolarityDiffTest` (Round preserve × arith/`*Value`/unsigned/signed, Abs transform both directions, Min/Max directional, RangeAsNumber grow-only, nested function-in-arith, the per-computation implicit validation, + a static-JAVA codegen tri-check) and multiplatform `interpreter.FunctionFillTest` (JVM+JS). Kernel fact: KERNEL-FINDINGS §1/§3 supplement "fill propagates through arithmetic" (extended to the value functions).

**Sweep delta (true before→after, workspaces re-run stashed vs restored).** workspaces held at **314** — every class UNCHANGED (`validateFull` 53/53/6, `compute` 153/39/10), kernel-mm **0**. **Zero net instance movement**: the function-wrapped-fill type corrections all land WITHIN `validateFull` instances that continue to diverge for a co-occurring reason (the `$`-correlation IG42, or a co-occurring kernel-only miss), so the instance-level tally doesn't move — the sweep counts diverging INSTANCES, not signatures, and a type-only fix cannot make the interpreter fire a code the kernel does not. As with IF69, this fix's value is the CORRECTNESS of the function-wrapped fill polarity (kernel-differential-proven, JVM+JS + codegen), not the corpus footprint. IG44 (compute-value) is untouched, as expected for a validateFull-only fix.

### IF72 — `DateRangesOverlap` is ANY-PAIR among ALL operands (a self-pair fires); `AtLeastOneDateRangeOverlaps` is scalar-vs-list; `Having` on a predicate rep-list arg is legal, keep-iff-TRUE with `$` trivially satisfied; polarity is filter-derived (FIXED 2026-07-08, graduated from IG42)

Two defects in the date-range-overlap predicates, both surfaced by the real-corpus sweep's "`CurrentRepetition(RuleGroup)` in a `$`-Having misses" class and root-caused by the BEST session (probe + source-characterized on BOTH kernel strategies): (1) the parser REJECTED a `Having` on a boolean predicate's rep-list arg (`DateRangesOverlap(X, Items* / Y Having (…))` → "expected ')'"), so the corpus idiom degraded; (2) the eval was a first-operand-vs-list test that deviated from the kernel's set semantics, and the two operators shared one impl though they differ exactly where the set semantics bites.

**The kernel semantics (clean-room from `RuntimeController.datumsBereicheUeberlappend` / `mindestensEinDatumsBereichUeberlappendFL` + `DatumBereich.ueberlappt`; the filter half from the codegen `CodeGenFilterCondition` → a compiled BOOLEAN):**

| aspect | `DateRangesOverlap(op0, op1, …)` | `AtLeastOneDateRangeOverlaps(scalar In list…)` |
|---|---|---|
| shape | **ANY-PAIR among ALL** operands' kept, filled cells — one growing set in operand order, fire on the first range overlapping any already-seen member; NO distinguished first operand | genuinely **scalar-vs-list**: the single unstarred first range vs each list cell; the scalar is NOT a set member |
| self-pair | YES — the same row's cell via a scalar ref AND via the star are two members → the self-pair fires (per-row `DateRangesOverlap(P, ../G* / P)` fires every filled row); a list-internal pair fires though arg0 is disjoint | only via the scalar's own row appearing IN the list; two LIST cells overlapping each other do NOT fire |
| empty/malformed cell | SKIPPED (`isWertAngegeben != TRUE → continue`); not unknown-suppression | SKIPPED; a not-filled/malformed SCALAR → FALSE (no fire), not UNKNOWN |
| invalid range (start > end) | never overlaps anything (`ueberlappt` false for either side invalid) | same |
| interval | CLOSED — `end1 ≥ start2 ∧ end2 ≥ start1` (touching overlaps) | same |
| polarity | filter-derived: OMISSION iff a filled cell of a `Having`-bearing operand was processed at/before the fire (`containsFilter` evolves in operand order), else VALUE | OMISSION iff the MATCHED list operand carries a `Having`, else VALUE |

**The `Having` keep-law (a rep-list arg of a PLAIN predicate — probed + codegen-confirmed; distinct from a quantifier's `Having`):** the kernel compiles the filter to a **boolean**, so **keep iff the filter is TRUE** (`<= 2` keeps rows 1–2; `x != x` drops all; a **malformed** operand → runtime-UNKNOWN → not-TRUE → **DROP** — locked, the one case the characterization left open). A `$`-correlation term has **no enclosing iteration** for a plain predicate's arg (unlike a quantifier's `Having`, whose star iterates), so the codegen cannot compile it and the whole comparison/predicate is **trivially TRUE** — that is why `!= $RuleGroup` and `== $RuleGroup` both KEEP every row (→ the self-pair fires 3), while `(x != x) And (== $RuleGroup)` = `FALSE And TRUE` = FALSE drops all. This is the crisp resolution of "`$`-UNKNOWN keeps but malformed-UNKNOWN drops": the `$` term is not runtime-UNKNOWN, it is codegen-degenerate-TRUE.

**A second `.having`-ignoring consumer swept (IF61 LOUD-degrade):** `FieldValuesNotUnique` — the kernel ACCEPTS + EVALUATES a `Having` on its starred operand (probed: `<=2` keeps rows 1–2, `>=2` keeps rows 2–3, changing distinctness), so it filters via the shared `filteredStarCells` (same keep-law). `SumOfProducts` — the kernel AUTHORING-REJECTS a filter (`MVK_WILDCARD_AT_LOWEST_LEVEL_REQUIRED`: the lowest repetition must be an unrestricted `*`), so it is parity-by-construction and keeps a defensive `rejectHaving` LOUD guard (never fires on a valid model; a stray filter is REPORTED, never silently ignored).

**The fix.** Parser: `readArgs` gains a `parseArg()` that attaches a trailing `Having` to WHATEVER starred operand precedes it (not only `arg0`). Eval: `overlapAny` (any-pair, one growing set, `containsFilter` in operand order) and a separate `atLeastOneOverlap` (scalar-vs-list, matched-container polarity) each return a `(truth, polarity)` `Res` so `evalPred` and `predType` share ONE scan; `rangesOverlap` gains the validity guard; a shared `filteredStarCells` honors the `Having` keep-law via `overlapFilterInterp` (a sub-interpreter with `outerUnbound=true`, so `evalCmp`/`evalPred` short-circuit a `$`-bearing term to TRUE); the two operators' catalog `polarity` flips to `null` (per-eval, computed in `predType`).

*Locked:* `adapter.laws.DateRangeOverlapSemanticsDiffTest` (13 cases: self-pair, list-internal pair, invalid-range, the 6 keep-law variants incl. malformed-drops, ALODRO scalar-vs-list + empty-scalar, both polarities — each a groovy-dynamic == java-static == interpreter tri-check) + `FieldValuesNotUniqueDiffTest`'s Having cases; multiplatform `interpreter.DateRangeOverlapTest` (JVM+JS, parser + eval). Kernel facts: KERNEL-FINDINGS §6 supplement "date-range overlap".

**Sweep delta.** workspaces held at **314** — the `DateRangesOverlap` `$`-Having rules now PARSE and AGREE with the kernel (the "`CurrentRepetition(RuleGroup)`-Having miss" class closed: no interp parse-failure skip, no new divergence; `validateFull both-sides-differ` still 53). Zero net instance movement (the IF69/IF71 pattern) — the corrected instances co-occur with the residual IG44/IG47 compute-side divergences, which IG42 does not touch. kernel-mm **0**.

### IF73 — the value functions return the kernel's fillable **0** on an EMPTY operand and FIRE: `Length` (grow-only), the date/time extractors + date diffs (symmetric) (FIXED 2026-07-08, graduated from IG46)

A DISTINCT root from the fill-polarity family (IF69/IF71): `Length` over an empty string, the date/time extractors (`Year/Month/Day/QuarterFromDate`, `Hours/Minutes/SecondsFromTime`), and the date diffs (`DifferenceInDays/Months/Years/Hours/Minutes/Seconds`) over an empty operand resolved to `Val.Unknown` in `Interpreter.evalFunc` (`dateComponent`/`timeComponent`/`epochOf`/`Length` → Unknown ⇒ the comparison is SUPPRESSED), whereas the kernel coerces an unspecified operand to a fillable **0** and FIRES. A false-NEGATIVE (the interpreter misses a kernel firing), not a fill-type mistype — and the mirror of the number column's documented empty-as-0 (`[Quantity] < 100` fires on an empty Quantity), which a DIRECT string/date comparison does NOT get (an empty string/date is suppressed), so these functions are the string/date families' "empty-as-0 backdoor".

**The kernel mechanism (source-derived, both strategies share the runtime):**

| function | kernel method | empty operand | fill |
|---|---|---|---|
| `Length(str)` | `BigDecimalUtils.laenge` | `VkString.length()` = **0** | **grow-only** (`kannGroesserWerden = nichtAngegeben`, `kannKleinerWerden = false`) |
| `Year/Month/Day/QuarterFromDate`, `Hours/Minutes/SecondsFromTime` | `getDatumsTeil` (Quarter: `getQuartalAusDatum`, month 0 → quarter 0) | `!hasValue()` → `DEFAULT_VALUE` = **0** | **symmetric** (`kannGroesser = kannKleiner = nichtAngegeben`) |
| `DifferenceInDays/Months/Years/Hours/Minutes/Seconds` | `getDatumsDiff` | EITHER operand `!hasValue()` → **0** | **symmetric** (`nichtAngegeben` of either) |

Precedence: a **MALFORMED** operand (`nichtPruefungsrelevant` — a format error, or a required-empty field) → `NICHT_PRUEF_REL_ZAHL` → the comparison stays SUPPRESSED (checked BEFORE the empty→0 in `getDatumsTeil`/`getDatumsDiff`, so malformed BEATS empty). The runtime methods are shared by both strategies (`laenge`'s own comment: "also used in the interpreter" / "used in the generated code") ⟹ codegen-parity.

**The fix.** `evalFunc` resolves an EMPTY check-relevant operand (`emptyCheckRelevant` — a plain field ref, not filled, not formally invalid) to `Val.Num(0)`: `Length` inline, `dateComponent`/`timeComponent` up front, the diffs via a shared `dateDiffEmpty(a, b)` (either malformed → Unknown; else either empty → 0; else compute) applied to `DifferenceInDays` + `dateDiff` + `dateTimeDiff`. `funcFill` gains `Length` as **grow-only** (`Fill.of(canGrow = leafOmission(arg), canShrink = false)`); the extractors + diffs keep the generic `else` leaf, which is already symmetric = the kernel's `getDatumsTeil`/`getDatumsDiff`. **Scope: DIRECT field operands** (the documented repros) — a composed operand like `Date(d,m,y)` with an empty part (an `Expr.Func`, not a field) keeps the current `HAS_EMPTY → Unknown` behavior; the kernel's VkDate empty-propagation through a construction is a separate, unreached corpus shape.

*Locked:* `adapter.laws.EmptyOperandFiringDiffTest` (Length/extractors/diffs empty-fires, malformed-suppress, filled-real, the `Length` grow-only asymmetry `>= 0 → VALUE` vs `< 5 → OMISSION`, the extractor/diff empty → OMISSION — each kernel-differential, on a content-bearing row) + multiplatform `interpreter.EmptyOperandFiringTest` (JVM+JS). Kernel facts: KERNEL-FINDINGS §6 supplement "the date family" (extended with the empty-operand-0 rule); `Length`'s empty-as-0 is the pre-existing `EmptyValueLawsTest`.

**Sweep delta.** workspaces held at **314** — every class unchanged. Zero net instance movement: the empty-firing corrections co-occur with other divergences, and the seeded corpus instances rarely present an empty operand in a firing position (the fields get seeded with values), so the instance tally doesn't move. As with IF69/IF71/IF72, the value is the differential-proven correctness (JVM+JS), not the corpus footprint. kernel-mm **0**.

### IF74 — a `Having` filter on a value-list quantifier operand: kernel-ACCEPTED on BOTH sides; drops rows before the per-cell classification; a Having-bearing operand forces OMISSION (FIXED 2026-07-08, graduated from IG48)

IF72 made `Having` parse on a plain predicate's starred rep-list arg (via `Parser.parseArg`), but the value-list quantifiers (`AtLeastOne/No/NotAllFieldValueIncludedInValueList`) parsed their fields + values sides through a SEPARATE path (`matchValueListQuant` used `parseExpr`, not `parseArg`), so a trailing `Having` failed to parse ("expected ')'") and the rule degraded — the same class as the IG42 miss (a kernel-legal filter silently degrading a rule). Recorded at the IF72 review gate as IG48; this session resolved it probe-first.

**Probe verdict — the ACCEPTED branch.** The kernel authoring ACCEPTS `Having` on a VLQ's fields side (all three ops) AND its field-ref values side (rulekit `RuleValidator` probe); the grammar bears it out (`feldListeUndWerteListenBedingung: name '(' entityListe inOp wertListenSpezifier ')'`, with `entityListe : entitySpec*` and each `entitySpec` carrying an optional `Mit`=`Having`; `wertListenSpezifier : entityListe | stringListe`, so a field-ref values list is filterable, a literal list is not). NOT parity-by-construction — a real gap.

**The kernel's filtered-VLQ semantics (behavioral characterization via the differential; both strategies share `RuntimeController`):**
- the `Having` **drops a row BEFORE the per-cell classification** — a dropped row contributes NOTHING (not to the membership test, `anyEmpty`, or `anyUnknown`), so only kept rows are classified (present / empty / malformed — the IF60 asymmetries unchanged over the kept subset);
- keep-law = the IF72 overlap-predicate one: keep iff the filter fires TRUE per row; a **malformed** filter operand → runtime-UNKNOWN → not-TRUE → DROP;
- **polarity is filter-derived:** a Having-bearing operand escalates a fired result to OMISSION (kernel `TRUE_AF`) **unconditionally** — even a filter that drops nothing, and even for `AtLeastOne` (VALUE when unfiltered). `No` still fires vacuously when the filter drops ALL rows (empty kept set → OMISSION).

**The fix.** Parser: `matchValueListQuant` routes every operand through `parseArg` (the same trailing-`Having` path aggregates/predicates use), so a filter rides a starred operand on either side. Interpreter: `vlqSide` classifies a filtered star over `ctx.rowContexts(group).filter { havingInterp(it).fires(having) }` (the `$` binds to the enclosing iteration, like `naiveStarQuantTally` — NOT the overlap predicates' unbound `$`), reusing the shared `classifyVlqCell`; the un-instantiated tail is unreachable under a filter (skipped), and `VlqSide.hasHaving` forces `valueListQuantType` to OMISSION on fire.

*Locked:* `adapter.laws.ValueListQuantifierHavingDiffTest` (13 cases across all three ops: filter-drops-before-membership, malformed-drops, empty-set guard, vacuous-No, NotAll-needs-a-kept-cell, values-side filter, and the filter-derived OMISSION incl. the drops-nothing case + an unfiltered-VALUE baseline — each a groovy-dynamic == java-static == interpreter tri-check) + multiplatform `interpreter.ValueListQuantifierHavingParseTest` (JVM+JS, both sides). Kernel facts: KERNEL-FINDINGS "shape-B QUANTIFIERS" supplement (the `Having` bullet).

**Sweep delta.** Deferred to the IG44 session's re-sweep (this REGULAR session did not re-run the workspaces sweep); the corpus footprint is expected small (the corrected idiom is a hand-authored filtered VLQ, uncommon), and — as with IF69/IF71/IF72/IF73 — the value is the differential-proven correctness (JVM+JS) + the closed parse-degrade class, not the instance tally.

### IF75 — a path's `/` separator and `*` wildcard are WHITESPACE-INSENSITIVE, matching the kernel's ANTLR lexer (FIXED 2026-07-08)

Surfaced while writing the IG48 lock: the hand-written parser treated a SPACED `Group* / Field` as arithmetic (` / ` → `ArithOp.DIV`, ` * ` → `ArithOp.MUL`) instead of a path, because `pathFrom` only continued a path across an **adjacent** `/`/`*`. The kernel's ANTLR lexer puts whitespace on a HIDDEN channel, so `Group* / Field` ≡ the space-free form (the kernel fact is homed: KERNEL-FINDINGS "Condition lexing") — the ANTLR parser is the **gold standard** (owner direction 2026-07-08), and this divergence silently mis-parsed a legal hand-authored path (the renderer itself emits no such spaces, so the corpus sweep — which checks parse SUCCESS, and a spaced path parses without ERROR as a bogus division — would not have flagged it).

**Why it is unambiguous to fix:** a BARE identifier is **never** an arithmetic operand — the grammar's arithmetic atoms (`einfacheOperation`) are bracketed `[entity]` (`feldWertOp: '[' entitySpec ']'`), constants, or named functions, so a `/`/`*` after a bare path segment is always a separator/wildcard; genuine div/mult (`[A] / [B]`) is between NON-path atoms and is untouched.

**The fix.** `Parser.pathFrom` continues a path across whitespace via a `skipWsAndMatch(c)` helper (skip ws, consume `c` or restore) for both the `/` separator and the `*` wildcard; the `[…]` value-ref branch skips whitespace after `[` (so `[ /abs]` / `[ ../rel]` resolve). *Locked:* multiplatform `interpreter.PathWhitespaceParseTest` (JVM+JS: spaced slash/wildcard in a starred + deep path, adjacent≡spaced, bracket-interior leading space, and a regression guard that `[A] / [B]` stays division). (Whitespace WITHIN a `..` up-run — `.. /seg`, `.. Name` — was the IG49 residual, closed by IF91.)

> **Sweep vocabulary (IF76–IF96, the IG44 compute campaign):** an **atom** is a per-pointer `(kernel-kind, interp-kind)` divergence pair, the kinds being the compute outcomes `VALUE`/`CLEARED`/`ERRORED`/`absent` ([CONFORMANCE-CORPUS-SPEC](CONFORMANCE-CORPUS-SPEC.md) §7). **CVV** = `kernel=CLEARED, interp=VALUE` (the interpreter wrongly computes where the kernel clears); **VCV** = `kernel=VALUE, interp=CLEARED` (the mirror — over-suppression). **Poison** = the kernel's compute-time invalidity propagation (IF85; the kernel fact lives in [KERNEL-FINDINGS](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md) §11). Full term index: [SEMANTICS-MAP "Vocabulary"](SEMANTICS-MAP.md).

### IF76 — a computation over a formally-INVALID operand CLEARS (the kernel's not-check-relevant rule); the IG44 root is operand invalidity, NOT tiered selection (FIXED 2026-07-08, graduated from IG44)

The dominant real-corpus compute divergence — **CLEARED-vs-VALUE** (the kernel clears a computed field where the interpreter computes a value) — was root-caused by the WI-T **model-level delta-debugger** (`adapter.laws.ModelShrinker`, the `FuzzDiagnostics.shrink` lifted to the MODEL): one diverging composite `_CDM` model (62 fields / 19 groups / 13 rules / 16 computations) was minimized IN MEMORY — dropping tiers, alternatives, preconditions, sibling fields/groups/rules while the divergence on the tracked pointer persisted — to **4 fields / 1 UNCONDITIONAL computation**: `Density = RoundDown([Inhabitants] * 1000000 / [Area], 2)`, where `Area` is declared with exactly 2 fractional digits (`min = max = 2`).

**The shrink overturned the IG44 hypothesis.** The row had characterized the class as **tiered** (345/372 CVV pointers carry a precondition/`commonPrecondition`) and blamed tier selection — but the minimal repro is **unconditional** (no tier), so tier-ness was a **co-occurrence, not the cause**. The real discriminator (differential over the seeds): the kernel CLEARS iff `Area` is **formally invalid** — `710` (0 fraction digits) / `-843.9` (1) / `-429` (0) all violate `minFractionalDigits = 2` and CLEAR; `257.71` / `-352.71` (exactly 2) COMPUTE. Every isolated own-domain probe matched because they used *valid* operands; the seeder's constraint-violating values are what exposed it.

**The kernel mechanism.** A formally-invalid field is **not check-relevant** (`nichtPruefungsrelevant`); an operation reading it is non-evaluable, so `compute()` produces nothing and the field is CLEARED (the same "unknown operand ⇒ suppressed" rule validation applies, on the compute path). This is EVERY `formalError` cause — a fraction-digit violation, an out-of-range value, a bad string length/pattern, a duplicate index, a required-empty cell — not one special case.

**The gap + fix (the shared root).** `DmInterpreter.compute` built its per-row `Interpreter` with the DEFAULT `invalid = { false }`, so a malformed operand resolved to its bogus value and computed, where `validate` built the SAME `Interpreter` WITH an `invalid = { formalErrorAt(…) != null }` gate (a formally-invalid `Expr.Field` → `Val.Unknown`, `Interpreter.kt` `evalExpr`). The fix threads that identical gate into `computeInstances` (building `dup`/`cache` once, mirroring `validate`), so a formally-invalid operand → `Val.Unknown` → the operation is non-evaluable → CLEARED. One locus, the whole class.

*Locked:* `adapter.laws.ComputeInvalidOperandDiffTest` (tri-check groovy-dynamic == java-static == interpreter): a fraction-constrained operand (valid 2-frac COMPUTES; 0/1-frac too-few and 3-frac too-many CLEAR) AND the **predicted second class** — a min/max-VALUE-constrained operand (in-range COMPUTES; below-min and above-max CLEAR) — each confirmed a real pre-fix divergence (RED without the gate). The `ModelShrinker` + its opt-in driver `ModelShrinkTest` are committed as reusable WI-T triage infrastructure. Kernel fact: KERNEL-FINDINGS §11 supplement "compute()" (the "FILLED but FORMALLY-INVALID operand CLEARS" paragraph).

**Scope — SUPERSEDED by [IF78] (the narrow scope was a wrong inference).** IF76 first scoped the gate to `Kind.NUMBER` operands and the value-domain `FormalErrorCode`s (min/max range + fraction-digit count — `NUMBER_OPERAND_CLEARS`), EXCLUDING the digit-length / format-sanity codes on the belief that "the corpus sweep shows the kernel COMPUTES with" them. **That belief was WRONG** — the direct clear-set differential (IF78) proves the kernel CLEARS a computation over an operand tripping EVERY number formal code (and string length/pattern too). IF78 generalized the gate to the kernel's actual rule (any formal error on a filled operand → clear, all kinds, via the shared `computeFormalErrorAt`) and removed `NUMBER_OPERAND_CLEARS`. An EMPTY operand (even required) still stays empty-as-0 (`nichtAngegeben`, not `nichtPruefungsrelevant`) — the differential's `requiredEmptyNumberOperand` guards it.

**Sweep delta (with the companion cascade fix IF77; license-safe `SweepReport` compute-atom instrumentation — per-differing-pointer kernel-kind vs interp-kind, no values/paths).** workspaces CVV (`kernel=CLEARED,interp=VALUE`) **372 → 236 (−136)**; total divergences **314 → 283**; the siblings `kernel=CLEARED,interp=absent` 383→124 and `kernel=CLEARED,interp=ERRORED` 146→27 also fell sharply (the fixes move interp from a bogus VALUE to CLEARED / the kernel's empty). operand-invalidity is **one contributor of a HETEROGENEOUS CVV class** — a residual ~236 remains from other causes (IG44 open; the `ModelShrinker` is committed for the re-drill). **One residual over-suppression: `kernel=VALUE,interp=CLEARED` 102 → 112 (+10).** IF76 attributed this to a `formalError` false-positive (tracked as IG50); **[IF78] DISCONFIRMED that** — a wide own-domain sweep (trait percent/amount × range × fraction × boundary values) found ZERO such false-positive, so the +10 is net movement within the heterogeneous over-suppression population (IG47), not a `formalError`-accuracy bug. IG50 was deleted. kernel-mm **0**.

### IF77 — a CLEARED computed field propagates to a downstream computation as EMPTY, not its stale STORED value (FIXED 2026-07-08, graduated from IG44)

A second IG44 CVV cause, found by delta-debugging the OVER-suppression direction (kernel VALUE, interp CLEARED) with the same `ModelShrinker`: when a computation CLEARS its target (a false precondition / no matching alternative), a DOWNSTREAM computation that reads it must see it **EMPTY**. The interpreter's cascade overlay (`OverlayContext`) recorded only computed **VALUES** (`overlay[key] = v` for `v !is Val.Unknown`), so a CLEARED target was ABSENT from the overlay and a dependent's read fell through to the STORED (seeded) cell — the stale pre-clear value. A dependent's `FieldFilled(dep)` then read the stale value as filled and computed where the kernel (seeing `dep` empty) clears via the false precondition; an operation read the stale value instead of empty-as-0.

**The fix.** `computeInstances` records a CLEARED target in a `cleared` set (the third cascade outcome, beside the VALUE `overlay` and the ERRORED `poisoned`); `OverlayContext` treats a cleared path as EMPTY — `filled` = false, `displayValue` = null, `field` = the kind-empty value (so a dependent number operand reads empty-as-0), `starCells` cell = null. So a dependent sees a cleared dependency exactly as the kernel does. This ALSO removed the bulk of the IF76 gate's over-suppression: a cleared dependency was being read stale-**and-invalid**, wrongly tripping the gate — now it reads empty (`filled` = false → the gate can't fire).

*Locked:* `adapter.laws.CascadeClearedDependencyDiffTest` (a two-link cascade `Mid = [Quantity]` gated `FieldFilled(Guard)`, then `Out = [Mid]` gated `FieldFilled(Mid)`: Guard-empty → Mid cleared → Out cleared, NOT the stale seed; Guard-filled → both compute; Quantity-empty → Mid = empty-as-0 = 0 → Out = 0). Kernel fact: KERNEL-FINDINGS §11 supplement "compute()" (the "precondition-CLEARED … propagates as EMPTY" paragraph).

**Boundary (carved by IF85, 2026-07-08):** this empty-reading cascade holds for a **precondition-false / no-matching-alternative clear** (a SILENT no-value — exactly what this lock's differential exercised). An **operand-invalidity** "clear" (IF76/IF78) is NOT this case: the kernel marks that target *invalid* ("fehlerhaft berechnet"), and a dependent's READ of it — value or presence — POISONS the dependent instead of reading empty (IF85). The kernel-source mechanism behind the empty read: the calc input strips every calculated field's input value (`CalculationCommand`, *"calculations always produce a new value anyways"*), so an un-computed, un-poisoned calculated field reads empty regardless of its seed.

### IF78 — a computation clears over ANY formally-invalid operand, not a value-domain subset; the compute gate is the SAME as validation's (FIXED 2026-07-08, generalizes IF76; graduated from the IG50 investigation + IG44 residual)

IF76 gated `compute` on operand invalidity but restricted the clear-set to five `Kind.NUMBER` value-domain codes (min/max range + fraction-digit count), EXCLUDING the digit-length / format-sanity codes on the belief the kernel "computes with" them. A direct **clear-set differential** (own-domain, one computation `RoundDown([N], 2)` per scenario, the kernel groovy-dynamic oracle) drove every number formal code as an operand and observed the kernel's compute outcome: **all fifteen CLEAR** — `zahlHatDezimalTrenner`, `stelligkeitZuLang`, `zahlZuvieleVorkommastellen`, `zahlHatFuehrendeNullen`, `zahlMitPlus`, `zahlNullEnthaeltVorzeichen`, `zahlZuKlein`/`zahlZuGross`, the fraction-digit codes, `zahlIstNull`, `zahlZuKurz`/`zahlZuLang`. A too-long STRING operand (`stringZuLang`) in a concat clears too. So the kernel's rule is uniform: **a FILLED operand that is "not check-relevant" (any formal error, any kind) makes a computation over it non-evaluable → CLEARED** — exactly the `computeFormalErrorAt` gate full validation already used.

**The gap + fix (the shared root).** `compute` built its `Interpreter` with the NARROW `numberOperandCleared` (the 5-code `NUMBER_OPERAND_CLEARS`), while `validate` used the full `formalErrorAt`. The fix makes `compute` use the SAME test — `operandNotCheckRelevant(path, ctx)` = *filled AND `computeFormalErrorAt(…) != null`* (any formal error, all kinds, over-limit, custom-type) — and DELETES `NUMBER_OPERAND_CLEARS`. `compute` and `validate` now share one operand-invalidity predicate. An EMPTY operand (even required) is still `false` → empty-as-0.

**Sweep footprint: ZERO net movement (honest — the seeder doesn't reach it).** workspaces CVV stayed **236**, over-suppression **112**, every compute-atom class byte-identical. `CorpusSeeding` generates constraint-*respecting* values — it trips the fraction-digit / range codes IF76 already cleared on, but not the codes this broadens to (integer-cap, zero-not-allowed, number-length, leading-zeros, plus/signed-zero, total-digits, the scale-0 decimal), so the seeded instances never exercise them (the IF71–IF73 pattern). The value is **differential-proven kernel-parity on a class of inputs a real document can carry** (a decimal in an integer field, an out-of-cap operand) and **correcting IF76's wrong "computes-with" rationale** — not a corpus-count reduction. The seeder's constraint-respecting bias is itself a harness limitation (noted on IG44).

**IG50 disconfirmed.** IG50 hypothesized the IF76 `+10` over-suppression was a `formalError` FALSE-POSITIVE on trait-bearing number values. A wide own-domain sweep (trait `percent`/`amount` × range × fraction × boundary values, kernel formal code vs interpreter) found **ZERO** over-suppression — not one value the interpreter flags that the kernel accepts. (`percent` has no value-domain effect anywhere in the kernel; only `amount` matters, and its integer-cap `zahlZuvieleVorkommastellen` was never in the clear-set.) So the `+10` is net movement within the heterogeneous over-suppression population (IG47), not a `formalError` bug; IG50 was deleted.

*Locked:* `adapter.laws.ComputeInvalidOperandDiffTest` (tri-check groovy-dynamic == java-static == interpreter) extended with the newly-covered classes — decimal-in-integer, leading-zeros, plus-sign, >15-digit, integer-cap, zero-not-allowed, number-too-long — each CLEARS; `interpreter … ComputeOperandInvalidityTest` pins the interpreter's compute outcome on JVM + JS. Kernel fact: KERNEL-FINDINGS §11 supplement "compute()".

### IF79 — a NumberType with no `maxFractionalDigits` is scale-0 (integer): the kernel's primitive-int default (FIXED 2026-07-08, graduated from the IG50 investigation)

The kernel's `NumberType.maxFractionalDigits` is a **primitive `int`** (kernel-md-a12internal), so a DM-JSON NumberType that OMITS it deserializes to **0** — the field is an INTEGER, and any decimal separator is `zahlHatDezimalTrenner`. The interpreter's `ModelLoader.scaleOf` returned **null** ("no scale constraint") for an absent `maxFractionalDigits`, so it permitted decimals — a divergence in BOTH directions: validation MISSED `zahlHatDezimalTrenner` (a rule referencing a decimal-valued integer field wrongly stayed live → under-suppression), and compute computed a value where the kernel CLEARS the operand (a CVV). An EXPLICIT `maxFractionalDigits: 0` was already handled; only the ABSENT case was wrong.

**The fix.** `scaleOf` returns `0` (not null) for a NumberType with an absent/non-int `maxFractionalDigits`. A null return now means only "not a number field" — a number is never scale-unconstrained. The formal-check logic (`scale == 0 && contains('.')` → `zahlHatDezimalTrenner`) was already correct; the loader was the sole locus.

**Sweep footprint: ZERO (the seeder produces integers here).** `SeedGenerator` uses `field.scale ?: 0`, so a no-`maxFractionalDigits` field was seeded with 0 decimals BOTH before (scale=null→0) and after (scale=0) the fix — the divergent decimal value never arises in the seeded corpus. Differential-proven correct regardless; a real document that stores a decimal in such a field now matches the kernel.

*Locked:* `interpreter … load.ModelLoaderTest.numberTypeWithoutMaxFractionalDigitsIsScaleZero` (loader → scale 0, JVM + JS); `adapter.laws.FieldConstraintFormalDiffTest.decimalInNoMaxFracFieldIsHatDezimalTrenner` (validate tri-check: a decimal in a no-`maxFractionalDigits` field → `zahlHatDezimalTrenner`, kernel == interpreter). Kernel fact: KERNEL-FINDINGS §5 ("a NUMBER field type is ALWAYS scale-bounded" — the primitive-int default).

### IF80 — `FirstFilledValue` over an empty selection computes the fillable 0 (value + OMISSION polarity), NOT cleared — the kernel's `NumberCombiner` (FIXED 2026-07-08, graduated from the IG44 residual re-drill)

The dominant IG44 residual — the 236-CVV `kernel=CLEARED,interp=VALUE` class — was re-drilled with the committed `ModelShrinker`. The minimal (both shrink directions converged on the SAME model+seed, because it diverges in both) reduced to two coupled computations: `CurrentPrice = FirstFilledValue(Pricing* / Price Having (FieldNotFilled(Start) And FieldNotFilled(End)))` and `Helper = if AtLeastOneFieldFilled(Pricing* / Price) And FieldNotFilled(CurrentPrice) → True`. The shrinker now also dumps the exact triggering instance (its filtered placements — OUR deterministic seeding, license-safe), since fresh re-seeding of the minimal misses the specific value pattern.

**The root (one mechanism, both pointers).** In the triggering instance NO Pricing row passes the Having filter (every row has Start+End filled), so `FirstFilledValue` sees an empty selection. **The kernel computes the fillable 0** (`NumberCombiner.value = ZAHL_0`, returned when `first == true` — no field added — with both `kannGroesser`/`kannKleiner`, so it FIRES/computes; the same empty-as-0 its `Sum`/`MinValue`/`MaxValue` siblings already give, confirmed from `NumberCombiner.getValue`). The interpreter's `firstFilledValue` returned `Val.Unknown` → CLEARED. `Helper` is a pure CASCADE of that root: the kernel's filled 0.00 makes `FieldNotFilled(CurrentPrice)` false → Helper cleared; the interpreter's cleared CurrentPrice makes it true → Helper wrongly computes. Confirmed with controlled own-domain scenarios (filter-fails / both-fail / filled-row-empty-price / no-rows → kernel 0, interp CLEARED — with VALID prices, so it is the empty selection, not operand invalidity).

**The fix (value + polarity).** `firstFilledValue` returns `Val.Num(0)` (the fillable 0) on a no-filled-value selection, preserving the partial-validation "a NON-relevant cell precedes the first filled → Unknown" case (IF42). The **polarity** matched too: `Document.firstStarRowEmpty` returned false for a no-filled-row star (`indexOfFirst == -1`), typing a fired `FirstFilledValue(empty) < n` VALUE; the kernel's `first == true` case is fully fillable → **OMISSION** (filling a cell raises the value off 0). Fixed to `indexOfFirst != 0` (no filled row OR an empty row precedes the first filled → OMISSION; only a first-row-filled value is fixed → VALUE). `MinValue`/`MaxValue`/`Sum` already returned the fillable 0 on empty (`Aggregates.min/max/sum([]) == 0`) — FirstFilledValue was the lone outlier of the family (the "predicted second instance" that was already correct).

**Sweep delta (workspaces):** total divergences **283 → 209 (−74)**; CVV `kernel=CLEARED,interp=VALUE` **236 → 141 (−95)**; the over-suppression `kernel=VALUE,interp=CLEARED` **112 → 96 (−16)**, `validateFull both-sides-differ` **53 → 29** and `kernel-only` **53 → 26**. The single dominant residual mechanism. (The polarity half added no further corpus movement — the affected fired rows co-occur — but is differentially required for rule-context parity.)

**Residual — the value-FORMAT (not the outcome), CLOSED by IF82.** The interpreter rendered a computed number canonical (`Dec.display` = `stripTrailingZeros`), so a `minFractionalDigits`-≥1 target got `0`/`10` where the kernel stores the padded `0.00`/`10.00`. Unmasked (not caused) by IF80 — a CLEARED-vs-VALUE pointer IF80 turns into two VALUEs now compares value STRINGS. Fixed in IF82: the kernel pads to the target's `minFractionalDigits` and BOTH compute paths render identically (the "two paths disagree" fear was a misdiagnosis).

*Locked:* `adapter.laws.FirstFilledValueDiffTest` (validate tri-check: `FirstFilledValue(empty) < 5` FIRES as OMISSION — distinguishing empty-as-0 from empty-as-unknown, which the prior `> 10` cases could not); `interpreter … FirstFilledValueEmptyTest` (compute outcome VALUE not CLEARED, incl. the Having-passes-nothing shape, JVM + JS). Kernel fact: KERNEL-FINDINGS §1/§3 supplement ("`FirstFilledValue` over an EMPTY selection returns its operand kind's OWN not-given value" — the per-kind refinement is IF83).

### IF81 — string concatenation over a FILLED-but-formally-invalid operand CLEARS; an EMPTY operand (even required) concatenates as "" (FIXED 2026-07-08, graduated from IG51)

The `+`-concatenation branch of `Interpreter.evalArith` maps every non-`Val.Txt` operand to `""` (`concatText`), so an EMPTY string field concatenates as `""` (kernel-probed `"" + " " + "Smith"` = `" Smith"`). But it could not distinguish **"Unknown because empty"** (→ `""`, correct) from **"Unknown because FORMALLY INVALID"** — a filled malformed value (too long, wrong pattern, …). The kernel treats a formally-invalid operand as not-check-relevant → the whole concatenation is non-evaluable → the computed field **CLEARS**. The interpreter wrongly computed a value with the invalid operand read as `""`.

**Kernel behavior (tri-check confirmed, e-commerce `[Code] + "-" + [Suffix]` over a `maxLength` string):**
- a FILLED operand `stringZuLang` (too long) — on the LEFT or the RIGHT of the concat → **CLEARED** (both kernel strategies);
- an OPTIONAL-empty operand → **VALUE** `-XY` (concatenates as `""`);
- a REQUIRED-empty operand → **VALUE** `-XY` too — the probe that was unknown when IG51 was filed: a required-empty string in a concat stays `""` (the `nichtAngegeben` empty-as-"" rule, NOT `nichtPruefungsrelevant`), it does **not** clear. So the gate is FILLED-only, exactly mirroring the compute operand-invalidity gate (IF78: empty — even required — stays empty-as-0).

**The fix.** In the concat branch, before mapping operands to text, if any concat operand (recursively through nested `+`) is a FILLED-but-invalid field (`ctxOf(e).filled(e.path) && invalid(e.path)`), return `Val.Unknown` — the whole concatenation is non-evaluable. `invalid` is the shared operand-invalidity gate (the compute path's `operandNotCheckRelevant` → `computeFormalErrorAt`; validation's `formalErrorAt`), and the `filled &&` guard excludes the required-empty case that validation's `invalid` would otherwise flag. Narrow — a formally-invalid string used as a *concat* operand; comparisons and other contexts already resolved a filled-invalid field to `Val.Unknown`. Untouched by the current corpus (the seeder respects string length/pattern), so no sweep movement; the value is the differential-proven correctness.

*Locked:* `adapter.laws.ConcatInvalidOperandDiffTest` (tri-check groovy == java-static == interpreter: too-long-left / too-long-right → CLEARED; optional-empty / required-empty → VALUE `-XY`; both-valid control). Fix in `commonMain` → JVM + JS. Kernel facts: KERNEL-FINDINGS §11 supplement "compute()" (the filled-invalid concat clear) + §2 supplement (empty concatenates as `""`).

### IF82 — a computed NUMBER stores PADDED to the target's `minFractionalDigits`; both kernel compute paths render identically (FIXED 2026-07-08, graduated from IG53)

The interpreter rendered a computed `Val.Num` via `Dec.display()` (canonical, `stripTrailingZeros`), so a target with `minFractionalDigits`≥1 got `10`/`0`/`5.5` where the kernel stores `10.00`/`0.00`/`5.50`. Unmasked by IF80: once `FirstFilledValue`-empty computes a value on both sides, the value STRINGS are compared and the format diff surfaces.

**Root — the kernel's shared store formatter** *(locus corrected 2026-07-09 by IF92: the store formatter is `CalculationController.handleBerechnetenWert`, whose fit-arm rule is `max(stripTrailingZeros().scale(), minFractionalDigits)` with **NO 15-digit cap** — the `NumberConversion.determineFractionalDigits` rule named here originally carries a `min(…, 15 − integerDigits)` cap the compute store path never applies; the two agree on every ≤ 15-digit value, so this finding's probe couldn't tell them apart)*. `decimalPlacesOptional` **defaults to false** (KERNEL-FINDINGS), so `minFractionalDigits` is honored. The rule is driven by **`minFractionalDigits`** (NOT max): a value with fewer natural fractional digits than the min is zero-padded to the min; more natural digits are kept.

**The IG53-filed "two paths disagree on trailing zeros" was a MISDIAGNOSIS.** Both the legacy V1 `compute(IDocument)` and the modern V2 `compute(DocumentV2, config)` use that ONE formatter and render bit-identically — a 13-case probe showed legacy == modern in every case (they cannot differ; the formatter is shared source). So there was no path divergence to reconcile; the BEST-gate "the modern path is normative" ruling resolves to the single shared rule.

**Corollary the fix also resolves.** A computed whole number into a `minFractionalDigits`≥1 field: before the fix the interpreter rendered `10`, then its OWN `computedOutcome` formal check flagged `zahlFalscheAnzahlNachkommastellen` (0 frac < min) → **ERRORED**. The kernel renders `10.00` → **VALUE**. So the fix corrects both the value string AND a VALUE-vs-ERRORED misclassification (an IG47 atom) in one move.

**The fix.** `Dec.display(minFractionalDigits: Int)` (expect + JVM BigDecimal + JS big.js `toFixed`) renders at `max(naturalScale, minFractionalDigits)`. `toComputed` renders `Val.Num` via it, passing `model.constraintsOf(targetPath).minFractionalDigits ?: 0`. With min 0 it equals `display()` (canonical) for every in-range value, so the existing compute tri-checks — all over `minFractionalDigits`-0 targets — are untouched. *(The JVM actual initially also applied the 15-digit `NumberConversion` cap, with JS eliding it as a "JS-edge" — IF92 removed the cap: the uncapped rule IS the kernel's store behavior, and the JS elision had been right all along. `toComputed` now rides the full `storedComputedNumber` store rule.)*

*Locked:* `adapter.laws.ComputedNumberScaleDiffTest` — a QUAD-check (legacy-compute == modern-computeModern == java-static == interpreter, and all == the expected stored form) across `minFractionalDigits` 0/1/2 over `Sum`/`FirstFilledValue`, incl. the zero, whole-number, one-trailing-zero, exact-scale, and keep-extra-digits boundaries. Kernel fact: KERNEL-FINDINGS "a computed NUMBER stores PADDED to minFractionalDigits". Fix in `commonMain` → JVM + JS.

### IF83 — `FirstFilledValue` over an EMPTY selection uses its operand kind's not-given value (NUMBER→0, DATE/STRING→CLEARED), not always `Num(0)` (FIXED 2026-07-08, graduated from the IG44 re-drill)

IF80 fixed the NUMBER `FirstFilledValue`-empty case (→ the fillable 0) but returned `Val.Num(0)` **unconditionally** for every kind. The kernel's `FirstValueCombiner` is a per-kind family (`FirstValueCombinerNumber`/`Date`/`DateRange`/`String`); on an empty selection each returns its OWN `notGivenValue()` — the fillable 0 (`NICHT_ANGEGEBENE_ZAHL`) only for NUMBER, a NOT-GIVEN value (`NOT_GIVEN_DATE`/`NICHT_ANGEGEBENER_STRING`/…) for the rest. So a DATE or STRING `FirstFilledValue` over an empty star returned `Num(0)` in the interpreter, which then hit `ValCompare.language(Txt(date/string), Num(0))` — the SAME crash class IF58 fixed for date min/max aggregates (a numeric identity where a date/string was expected, a type mixture no legal kernel input produces).
